Question 1674
A 26-year-old woman comes to the clinic because of painful lumps on her buttocks and upper thighs that drain and leave scars. They began soon after puberty and have slowly worsened. She smokes and has obesity. Cultures of the drainage have been negative. The affected area is shown in the attached image. Which of the following is the first known step in the process that leads to her lesions?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-13.jpg)
A. Bacterial infection of eccrine sweat glands
B. Occlusion of pilosebaceous units in skin with apocrine glands
C. Hyperkeratosis in the upper sebaceous follicle forming a microcomedo
D. T cell mediated autoimmune destruction of hair follicles
E. Overgrowth of Malassezia yeast on the skin
Answer: B. Occlusion of pilosebaceous units in skin with apocrine glands
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Hidradenitis suppurativa
Why this is correct:
Draining nodules and scars on the buttocks after puberty in a smoker with obesity are hidradenitis suppurativa. After an unknown trigger, pilosebaceous units in apocrine gland skin are occluded, leading to apocrine gland inflammation, nodules and sterile abscesses, and then sinus tracts and scarring.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The abscesses of hidradenitis suppurativa are sterile, and the disease involves apocrine, not eccrine, glands.
C. Microcomedo formation is the first step in acne vulgaris.
D. T cell destruction of hair follicles is alopecia areata.
E. Malassezia overgrowth causes tinea versicolor.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation and diagnosis of acne vulgaris, rosacea and hidradenitis suppurativa and how treatments vary.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1675
A 34-year-old woman with hidradenitis suppurativa comes to the clinic because of worsening pain and drainage in her right axilla over several years. The affected axilla is shown in the attached image. Which of the following best describes the stage of her disease?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-14.jpg)
A. Hurley stage I, because nodules and abscesses are present
B. Hurley stage II, because sinus tracts and scars are few
C. Hurley stage III, because sinus tracts are multiple and interconnected
D. Hurley stage I, because sinus tracts and scars are absent
E. Hurley stage II, because disease is limited to one axilla
Answer: C. Hurley stage III, because sinus tracts are multiple and interconnected
Order: First
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Hurley staging and treatment of hidradenitis suppurativa
Why this is correct:
The axilla shows extensive interconnected sinus tracts with scarring and drainage across the region, which is Hurley stage III.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Nodules and abscesses occur in every stage, so they don't define stage I.
B. Stage II has only a few sinus tracts and scars, not the extensive tracts shown.
D. Sinus tracts and scars are clearly present here.
E. Hurley stage depends on sinus tracts and scars, not on how many sites are involved.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation and diagnosis of acne vulgaris, rosacea and hidradenitis suppurativa and how treatments vary.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1676
A 21-year-old woman comes to the clinic because of recurrent painful bumps in her left axilla for 6 months. Each one lasts 1 to 2 weeks and then settles. She has obesity and smokes. Her axilla is shown in the attached image. Which of the following is the most appropriate treatment in addition to weight loss and smoking cessation?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-15.jpg)
A. Infliximab
B. Surgical excision
C. Adalimumab
D. Oral isotretinoin
E. Topical antibiotics and antiseptic washes
Answer: E. Topical antibiotics and antiseptic washes
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Hurley staging and treatment of hidradenitis suppurativa; Hidradenitis suppurativa
Why this is correct:
A few inflammatory nodules in the axilla with no sinus tracts or scars is Hurley stage I hidradenitis suppurativa. Stage I is treated with topical antibiotics, antiseptic washes, intralesional steroids, or oral antibiotics, along with weight loss and smoking cessation.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Infliximab is a biologic for stage III disease.
B. Surgical excision is used for stage II and III disease.
C. Adalimumab is used for stage II and III disease.
D. Isotretinoin is a treatment for nodulocystic acne.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation and diagnosis of acne vulgaris, rosacea and hidradenitis suppurativa and how treatments vary.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1677
A 20-year-old man comes to the clinic because his palms are constantly wet, which makes it hard to write and shake hands. The sweating affects both hands equally and stops while he sleeps. His father has the same problem. He takes no medications and has no fever or other symptoms. His palms are shown in the attached image. Which of the following is required to diagnose primary hyperhidrosis in this patient?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-16.jpg)
A. The sweating has been present for at least 6 months
B. The sweating occurs at least once a week
C. The sweating also involves the soles and axillae
D. The sweating is generalized rather than focal
E. The sweating began after the age of 25
Answer: A. The sweating has been present for at least 6 months
Order: Second
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Primary hyperhidrosis diagnostic criteria
Why this is correct:
Primary hyperhidrosis requires focal, visible excess sweating present for at least 6 months, no apparent secondary cause, and at least two supporting features. He already has several supporting features (bilateral and symmetric, impairs daily activities, stops during sleep, family history), so the missing required item is duration.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. At least one episode per week is only a supporting feature, and he already has more than two.
C. Primary disease favors the palms, soles, and axillae, but all three sites aren't required.
D. The criteria require focal, not generalized, sweating.
E. Onset before age 25 is the supporting feature.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, and treatment of hyperhidrosis.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1678
A 24-year-old woman with primary hyperhidrosis of the axillae has had little improvement with topical aluminum chloride 20%. She has no other medical problems and takes no other medications. Oral glycopyrrolate is started. Which of the following best describes the mechanism of this drug in her condition?
A. Physically occludes eccrine sweat glands
B. Blocks acetylcholine release
C. Constricts blood vessels in the skin
D. Blocks muscarinic receptors on eccrine sweat glands
E. Decreases androgen stimulation of glands
Answer: D. Blocks muscarinic receptors on eccrine sweat glands
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Primary hyperhidrosis treatment
Why this is correct:
Glycopyrrolate and oxybutynin are topical or oral anticholinergics that block muscarinic receptors on eccrine sweat glands.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Physical occlusion of eccrine glands is how aluminum chloride works, and it already failed.
B. Blocking acetylcholine release is how botulinum toxin injections work.
C. Blood vessel constriction is how brimonidine works in rosacea.
E. Decreasing androgen stimulation is how anti-androgens like spironolactone and finasteride work.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, and treatment of hyperhidrosis.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1679
A 36-year-old woman comes to the clinic because of excessive sweating all over her body for 3 months. She never had a sweating problem before this year. She takes no medications and has no fever. No one in her family has had excessive sweating. Her thyroid hormone level is high. Which of the following best explains her sweating?
A. Primary hyperhidrosis
B. Secondary hyperhidrosis from hyperthyroidism
C. Secondary hyperhidrosis from a medication
D. Secondary hyperhidrosis from a febrile illness
E. Secondary hyperhidrosis from lymphoma
Answer: B. Secondary hyperhidrosis from hyperthyroidism
Order: Second
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Hyperhidrosis: primary vs. secondary
Why this is correct:
New, generalized sweating with a high thyroid hormone level points to secondary hyperhidrosis, which is related to an underlying disease such as hyperthyroidism.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Primary hyperhidrosis has no underlying disease and favors the palms, soles, and axillae.
C. She takes no medications, so a drug cause (such as SSRIs or opioids) is unlikely.
D. She has no fever.
E. Her high thyroid hormone level points to hyperthyroidism rather than lymphoma.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, and treatment of hyperhidrosis.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1680
A 32-year-old woman comes to the clinic because of white patches on the backs of her hands that have spread over a year. She also has white patches around her mouth. The patches are not scaly or itchy. Her hand is shown in the attached image. Which of the following conditions is most strongly associated with her skin disorder?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-17.jpg)
A. Hidradenitis suppurativa
B. Lymphoma
C. Graves disease
D. Telogen effluvium
E. Tinea capitis
Answer: C. Graves disease
Order: Third
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Vitiligo
Why this is correct:
Sharply bordered depigmented patches on the hands and perioral skin are vitiligo, caused by autoimmunity targeting melanocytes. It's associated with other autoimmune diseases: alopecia areata, autoimmune hypothyroidism, and Graves disease.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Hidradenitis suppurativa is linked to obesity and smoking, not vitiligo.
B. Lymphoma is listed as a cause of secondary hyperhidrosis.
D. Telogen effluvium is hair shedding after a stressor, not an autoimmune disease.
E. Tinea capitis is a dermatophyte infection, not an autoimmune association.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation and diagnosis of common disorders of pigment such as vitiligo, pityriasis alba, and tinea versicolor and approaches to therapy.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1681
A 6-year-old boy is brought to the clinic because of pale patches on his cheeks that have become more noticeable this summer. The patches are round with slight scale and don't bother him. He has a history of eczema. His face is shown in the attached image. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-18.jpg)
A. Vitiligo
B. Tinea versicolor
C. Postinflammatory hyperpigmentation
D. Papulopustular rosacea
E. Pityriasis alba
Answer: E. Pityriasis alba
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Pityriasis alba; Tinea versicolor
Why this is correct:
Hypopigmented, round patches with slight scale on the face of a young child with atopic dermatitis, worse in summer, are pityriasis alba.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Vitiligo is depigmentation (white, not pale) and is not described as scaly.
B. Tinea versicolor is usually on the chest and back of teenagers and young adults.
C. Postinflammatory hyperpigmentation causes dark spots after acne lesions heal.
D. Papulopustular rosacea causes red papules and pustules on the central face.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation and diagnosis of common disorders of pigment such as vitiligo, pityriasis alba, and tinea versicolor and approaches to therapy.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1682
A 17-year-old boy comes to the clinic because of many small pale spots on his upper back and shoulders. They became more obvious after he got a tan this summer. They don't itch, and he is otherwise healthy. His shoulder is shown in the attached image. Which of the following best explains the pale color of the spots?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-19.jpg)
A. Yeast overgrowth that decreases melanin synthesis
B. Autoimmunity that targets melanocytes
C. A mild variant of atopic dermatitis
D. Dermatophyte infection of hair follicles
E. Healed inflammation that leaves pigment behind
Answer: A. Yeast overgrowth that decreases melanin synthesis
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Tinea versicolor
Why this is correct:
Hypopigmented patches on the back and shoulders of a teenager that stand out more in summer are tinea versicolor. Overgrowth of Malassezia yeast decreases melanin synthesis and tanning of the underlying skin.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Autoimmunity against melanocytes causes vitiligo, which is depigmented and favors the hands, feet, and perioral and periocular skin.
C. A mild variant of atopic dermatitis is pityriasis alba, which affects the face of young children.
D. Dermatophyte infection of hair follicles is tinea capitis.
E. Postinflammatory pigment change after acne makes the skin darker, not paler.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation and diagnosis of common disorders of pigment such as vitiligo, pityriasis alba, and tinea versicolor and approaches to therapy.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1683
A 9-year-old girl is brought to the clinic because of a round patch of hair loss on her scalp noticed 3 weeks ago. The patch doesn't itch, and the skin is smooth with no redness or scale. Her mother had a similar patch as a child. The scalp is shown in the attached image. Which of the following best explains her hair loss?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-20.jpg)
A. Dermatophyte infection of hair follicles
B. Androgen-driven miniaturization of hair follicles
C. More rapid hair cycling with increased shedding
D. T cell mediated autoimmune destruction of hair follicles
E. Autoimmunity that targets melanocytes
Answer: D. T cell mediated autoimmune destruction of hair follicles
Order: Second
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Alopecia areata
Why this is correct:
A circular, smooth, asymptomatic patch of hair loss with no erythema or scale in a child with a family history is alopecia areata, caused by T cell mediated autoimmune destruction of hair follicles.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Dermatophyte infection is tinea capitis, which has scaling and itching.
B. Androgen-driven miniaturization causes androgenic alopecia in a frontal and vertex or part-line pattern.
C. Rapid hair cycling causes the diffuse loss of telogen effluvium.
E. Autoimmunity against melanocytes causes vitiligo.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, pathophysiology, and treatment of common disorders of hair such as alopecia areata, tinea capitis, telogen effluvium, and androgenic alopecia.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1684
A 24-year-old man comes to the clinic because he has lost all of the hair on his scalp over the past year. It started as round, smooth patches that spread and merged. His eyebrows, eyelashes, beard, and body hair are normal. The scalp has no scale. His scalp is shown in the attached image. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-21.jpg)
A. Alopecia universalis
B. Alopecia totalis
C. Androgenic alopecia
D. Telogen effluvium
E. Tinea capitis
Answer: B. Alopecia totalis
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Alopecia areata
Why this is correct:
Alopecia totalis is the alopecia areata variant with loss of all scalp hair, which fits loss of the entire scalp with normal body hair after smooth patches.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Alopecia universalis is loss of all body hair, but his body hair is normal.
C. Androgenic alopecia in men causes loss of the frontal scalp and vertex, not round patches.
D. Telogen effluvium causes diffuse, sudden shedding after a stressful event and self-resolves.
E. Tinea capitis has scaling and itching.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, pathophysiology, and treatment of common disorders of hair such as alopecia areata, tinea capitis, telogen effluvium, and androgenic alopecia.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1685
A 30-year-old woman comes to the clinic because of hair loss that has spread rapidly over the past 2 months, starting as smooth, round patches. She has no scaling or itching. She has vitiligo. Her scalp is shown in the attached image. Which of the following is the most appropriate treatment?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-22.jpg)
A. Topical calcineurin inhibitor
B. Oral terbinafine for 4 to 8 weeks
C. Tofacitinib
D. Topical minoxidil
E. Intralesional corticosteroids
Answer: C. Tofacitinib
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Alopecia areata treatment and prognosis; Alopecia areata
Why this is correct:
Rapidly spreading smooth patches in a patient with vitiligo are alopecia areata, here widespread and rapidly progressive. That calls for systemic immunosuppression to decrease T cell function, with oral steroids or tofacitinib (a JAK/STAT inhibitor).
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Topical calcineurin inhibitors are for localized disease.
B. Oral terbinafine treats tinea capitis, which has scaling and itching.
D. Topical minoxidil treats androgenic alopecia.
E. Intralesional corticosteroids are for localized disease.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, pathophysiology, and treatment of common disorders of hair such as alopecia areata, tinea capitis, telogen effluvium, and androgenic alopecia.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1686
A 7-year-old boy is brought to the clinic because of an itchy patch of thinning hair on his scalp for 1 month. He scratches it often, and the area is covered with white scale. He is otherwise healthy. His scalp is shown in the attached image. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-23.jpg)
A. Alopecia areata
B. Telogen effluvium
C. Androgenic alopecia
D. Pityriasis alba
E. Tinea capitis
Answer: E. Tinea capitis
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Tinea capitis; Hair loss disorders compared
Why this is correct:
A patch of hair loss with scaling and itching is tinea capitis, a dermatophyte infection of hair follicles that can mimic alopecia areata.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Alopecia areata is smooth, with no scaling, and asymptomatic.
B. Telogen effluvium is diffuse loss, not a scaly patch.
C. Androgenic alopecia is a progressive pattern of frontal and vertex loss or a widening part.
D. Pityriasis alba causes hypopigmented patches on the face, not hair loss.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, pathophysiology, and treatment of common disorders of hair such as alopecia areata, tinea capitis, telogen effluvium, and androgenic alopecia.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1687
A 6-year-old girl is brought to the clinic because of an itchy, scaly patch of hair loss on her scalp. A scraping of the scale is examined under the microscope after potassium hydroxide is applied, and the result is shown in the attached image. Which of the following is the most appropriate treatment?
![KOH preparation](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-24.jpg)
A. Oral terbinafine for 4 to 8 weeks
B. Intralesional corticosteroids
C. Topical calcineurin inhibitor
D. Tofacitinib
E. Topical minoxidil
Answer: A. Oral terbinafine for 4 to 8 weeks
Order: Third
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Tinea capitis
Why this is correct:
Branching fungal hyphae on the KOH scraping confirm tinea capitis, which is treated with oral antifungals (terbinafine, griseofulvin, or fluconazole) for 4 to 8 weeks.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Intralesional corticosteroids treat localized alopecia areata.
C. Topical calcineurin inhibitors treat localized alopecia areata.
D. Tofacitinib treats widespread or rapidly progressive alopecia areata.
E. Topical minoxidil treats androgenic alopecia.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, pathophysiology, and treatment of common disorders of hair such as alopecia areata, tinea capitis, telogen effluvium, and androgenic alopecia.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1688
A 31-year-old woman comes to the clinic because large amounts of hair have been coming out in her brush and in the shower for the past month. The loss is spread evenly over her scalp, with no patches, scale, or itching. She delivered her first child 3 months ago. Which of the following best explains her hair loss?
A. T cell mediated destruction of hair follicles
B. Dermatophyte infection of hair follicles
C. Androgen-driven miniaturization of hair follicles
D. More rapid hair cycling with increased shedding
E. Normal loss of about 100 hairs a day
Answer: D. More rapid hair cycling with increased shedding
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Telogen effluvium; Hair loss disorders compared
Why this is correct:
Diffuse, sudden hair loss after pregnancy is telogen effluvium, which is caused by more rapid cycling of hair and increased shedding. It self-resolves in 6 to 12 months.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. T cell destruction of follicles causes the circular patches of alopecia areata.
B. Dermatophyte infection causes tinea capitis, with scaling and itching.
C. Androgenic miniaturization causes a slow, progressive pattern, such as a widening part in women.
E. About 100 hairs a day is normal loss, and her shedding is clearly increased.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, pathophysiology, and treatment of common disorders of hair such as alopecia areata, tinea capitis, telogen effluvium, and androgenic alopecia.

MSK / Week 13 / Spectrum of Acneiform Disorders, Pigmentation, and Hair
Question 1689
A 52-year-old woman comes to the clinic because her hair has been thinning gradually over several years. Her mother had similar thinning. She has no patches, scale, or itching. Views of the top of the scalp in her pattern of hair loss are shown in the attached image. Topical minoxidil is started. Which of the following best describes how this drug helps her condition?
![Clinical photo](library/images/msk-week-13/questions/q-derm-25.jpg)
A. Decreases androgen stimulation of hair follicles
B. Increases blood circulation to follicles to prevent miniaturization
C. Decreases T cell function through JAK/STAT inhibition
D. Kills the dermatophytes that infect hair follicles
E. Slows the rapid hair cycling that increases shedding
Answer: B. Increases blood circulation to follicles to prevent miniaturization
Order: Second
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Androgenic alopecia
Why this is correct:
Gradual, familial widening of the part line in a woman is androgenic alopecia, where androgens cause progressive miniaturization of hair follicles. Topical minoxidil increases blood circulation to hair follicles to prevent miniaturization.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Decreasing androgen stimulation is how the anti-androgens (finasteride, spironolactone, dutasteride) work.
C. JAK/STAT inhibition is how tofacitinib treats widespread alopecia areata.
D. Oral antifungals kill dermatophytes in tinea capitis.
E. Rapid hair cycling causes telogen effluvium, which self-resolves without a specific treatment.
Objective: Identify and discuss the clinical presentation, diagnosis, pathophysiology, and treatment of common disorders of hair such as alopecia areata, tinea capitis, telogen effluvium, and androgenic alopecia.

MSK / Week 13 / Anatomy Lab: Vertebral Column and Back

Question 1690
A first-year medical student reviews the prosection of the superficial back shown in the attached image during an anatomy practical. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-01-superficial-back.png)
A. Rhomboid major muscle
B. Latissimus dorsi muscle
C. Splenius muscle
D. Trapezius muscle
E. Levator scapulae muscle
Answer: D. Trapezius muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Trapezius muscle; Back muscle layers and their innervation
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the large, triangular, most superficial muscle of the upper back, which runs from the occipital bone, nuchal ligament, and spinous processes of C7 to T12 out to the lateral clavicle, acromion, and spine of the scapula.
Its fibers converge on the shoulder from a long midline origin, which gives the diamond shape seen when both sides are viewed together.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Rhomboid major lies deep to trapezius and can't be seen until trapezius is reflected.
B. Latissimus dorsi is the broad, fan-shaped muscle of the lower back (pin 4), not the upper triangular sheet.
C. Splenius is a deep muscle of the posterior neck, covered by trapezius here.
E. Levator scapulae is a thin strap deep to trapezius in the neck, hidden in this superficial view.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1691
A 34-year-old woman comes to the clinic 3 weeks after excision of a lymph node from the posterior triangle of her neck. She has a dull ache over her right shoulder and can't shrug her right shoulder against the examiner's resistance, though she can shrug the left normally. Sensation over the neck is intact. Injury to a nerve during the procedure is suspected. Which of the following nerves was most likely injured?
A. Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI)
B. Posterior rami of the cervical spinal nerves
C. Thoracodorsal nerve
D. Dorsal scapular nerve
E. Anterior rami of C3 and C4
Answer: A. Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI)
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Trapezius muscle
Why this is correct:
Shrugging against resistance is the clinical test for the spinal accessory nerve, because CN XI provides motor innervation to trapezius, the muscle that elevates the scapula.
Loss of a shrug on one side after neck surgery points to injury of CN XI on that side.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Posterior rami supply the deep (intrinsic) back muscles, not trapezius.
C. The thoracodorsal nerve supplies latissimus dorsi, which extends, adducts, and medially rotates the arm rather than elevating the scapula.
D. The dorsal scapular nerve supplies levator scapulae and the rhomboids; it doesn't provide the motor supply to trapezius, which is the main elevator tested by a shrug.
E. The anterior rami of C3 and C4 carry pain and proprioception from trapezius, not its motor supply.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1692
A researcher studies the bony attachments of the muscle marked by pin 2 in the attached image of a dissection in which the muscle is being lifted at its inferolateral border. Which of the following is a distal attachment of this muscle?
![Cadaveric dissection of the upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-02-trapezius-elevated.png)
A. Spine of the scapula
B. Superior borders of ribs 2 to 4
C. Superior angle of the scapula
D. Medial border of the scapula inferior to the spine
E. Floor of the intertubercular groove of the humerus
Answer: A. Spine of the scapula
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Trapezius muscle; Back muscle layers and their innervation
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks trapezius, which inserts on the lateral third of the clavicle, the acromion, and the spine of the scapula.
Its proximal attachments are the occipital bone, the nuchal ligament, and the spinous processes of C7 to T12.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Ribs 2 to 4 receive serratus posterior superior.
C. The superior angle and medial border above the spine receive levator scapulae.
D. The medial border inferior to the spine is the distal attachment of rhomboid major.
E. The floor of the intertubercular groove is the distal attachment of latissimus dorsi.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1693
In the dissection shown in the attached image, the muscle marked by pin 1 has been detached from the spine of the scapula and reflected. The orientation key is shown in the lower left. Which of the following best identifies the muscle marked by pin 1?
![Cadaveric dissection of the shoulder region, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-04-trapezius-scapular-spine.png)
A. Right latissimus dorsi muscle
B. Left latissimus dorsi muscle
C. Left levator scapulae muscle
D. Right trapezius muscle
E. Left trapezius muscle
Answer: E. Left trapezius muscle
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Trapezius muscle; Back muscle layers and their innervation
Why this is correct:
The muscle being reflected off the spine of the scapula and acromion is trapezius, since those are its distal attachments.
The orientation key shows left on the left of the image in this posterior view, and the acromion (pin 2) sits at the left edge of the dissection, so this is the left shoulder.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Latissimus dorsi doesn't attach to the scapular spine, and the side is also wrong.
B. Latissimus dorsi is the muscle marked by pin 4, inferior to the scapula, and it doesn't attach to the spine of the scapula.
C. Levator scapulae attaches to the superior angle and medial border, not the spine and acromion.
D. The orientation key places this dissection on the body's left side, not the right.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1694
A 22-year-old competitive swimmer is evaluated for weakness in his stroke. When asked to bring his abducted arm down to his side and then pull it backward against resistance, he is weak on the right, and internal rotation of the right arm is also weak. Shoulder shrug is normal. Injury to which of the following structures most likely explains these findings?
![Cadaveric dissection of the axilla and back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-10-latissimus-nv-bundle.png)
A. Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI), not shown
B. Structure at pin 3
C. Structure at pin 1
D. Structure at pin 2
E. Dorsal scapular nerve, not shown
Answer: B. Structure at pin 3
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Latissimus dorsi muscle
Why this is correct:
Weak adduction, extension, and medial rotation of the arm with a normal shrug points to latissimus dorsi, which is innervated by the thoracodorsal nerve.
Pin 3 marks the thoracodorsal nerve within the neurovascular bundle entering the deep surface of the reflected latissimus dorsi.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. CN XI supplies trapezius, and injury would cause a weak shrug, which is normal here.
C. Pin 1 is the thoracodorsal vein, which drains the muscle and wouldn't produce weakness.
D. Pin 2 is the thoracodorsal artery, the blood supply to latissimus dorsi rather than its motor supply.
E. The dorsal scapular nerve supplies the rhomboids and levator scapulae, which move the scapula rather than the arm.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1695
A first-year medical student reviews the dissection shown in the attached image, in which trapezius has been reflected. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-06-trapezius-reflected.png)
A. Thoracolumbar fascia
B. Rhomboid major muscle
C. Iliocostalis muscle
D. Latissimus dorsi muscle
E. Serratus posterior inferior muscle
Answer: D. Latissimus dorsi muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Latissimus dorsi muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the broad, fan-shaped superficial muscle of the lower back that arises from the lower thoracic spinous processes, thoracolumbar fascia, and iliac crest and sweeps toward the humerus.
This is latissimus dorsi, sometimes called the climber's or rower's muscle.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The thoracolumbar fascia is the aponeurotic sheet at pin 3, not the muscle belly at pin 2.
B. Rhomboid major sits higher, between the thoracic spinous processes and the medial border of the scapula.
C. Iliocostalis is part of the deep erector spinae group and is covered by the superficial and intermediate layers.
E. Serratus posterior inferior is a thin intermediate muscle deep to latissimus dorsi, not visible until latissimus is reflected.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1696
A researcher studies the muscle marked by pin 2 in the attached image. Its tendon is traced to the humerus. Where does this muscle attach distally?
![Cadaveric dissection of the lower back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-07-latissimus-elevated.png)
A. Lateral third of the clavicle
B. Medial border of the scapula between the spine and the inferior angle
C. Inferior borders of ribs 8 to 12
D. Floor of the intertubercular groove of the humerus
E. Acromion process of the scapula
Answer: D. Floor of the intertubercular groove of the humerus
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Latissimus dorsi muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks latissimus dorsi, which runs from the lower thoracic spinous processes, thoracolumbar fascia, and iliac crest to the floor of the intertubercular groove of the humerus.
That humeral attachment is why it adducts, extends, and medially rotates the arm.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The lateral third of the clavicle receives trapezius.
B. The medial border between the spine and inferior angle receives rhomboid major.
C. The inferior borders of ribs 8 to 12 receive serratus posterior inferior.
E. The acromion also receives trapezius.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1697
During dissection, a student reflects the muscle marked by pin 4 in the attached image and finds the neurovascular bundle on its deep surface. Which of the following movements would be weakest if the nerve in this bundle were cut?
![Cadaveric dissection of the axilla and back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-10-latissimus-nv-bundle.png)
A. Elevation of the scapula
B. Retraction of the scapula
C. Abduction and lateral rotation of the arm
D. Lateral flexion of the vertebral column
E. Adduction, extension, and medial rotation of the arm
Answer: E. Adduction, extension, and medial rotation of the arm
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Latissimus dorsi muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks latissimus dorsi, and the nerve in its neurovascular bundle is the thoracodorsal nerve.
Cutting that nerve paralyzes latissimus dorsi, so its actions (adduction, extension, and medial rotation of the arm) would be weakest.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Scapular elevation comes from trapezius (CN XI) and levator scapulae (dorsal scapular nerve).
B. Retraction comes from trapezius and the rhomboids.
C. Abduction and lateral rotation are the opposite of what latissimus dorsi does.
D. Lateral flexion of the vertebral column is an action of the erector spinae, supplied by posterior rami.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1698
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image. Trapezius has been reflected to expose the muscles beneath it on the right side. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 6?
![Cadaveric dissection of the right upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-11-rhomboids-levator.png)
A. Levator scapulae muscle
B. Rhomboid minor muscle
C. Serratus posterior superior muscle
D. Latissimus dorsi muscle
E. Rhomboid major muscle
Answer: E. Rhomboid major muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 6 marks the wide, flat muscle running from the upper thoracic spinous processes to the medial border of the scapula below the spine, which is rhomboid major.
Rhomboid major is wider and more inferior than rhomboid minor, which sits just above it.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Levator scapulae is the strap-like muscle running up into the neck toward the cervical transverse processes (pin 3).
B. Rhomboid minor is the narrower, thicker muscle just superior to rhomboid major (pin 4).
C. Serratus posterior superior lies deep to the rhomboids and isn't exposed in this view.
D. Latissimus dorsi is at the inferior edge of the field (pin 8).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1699
A researcher studies the muscle marked by pin 5 in the attached image, which is being cut along its medial attachment. Which of the following describes its proximal attachment and action?
![Cadaveric dissection of the upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-12-rhomboids-both-sides.png)
A. Spinous processes of T2 to T5; retracts the scapula
B. Spinous processes of C7 to T1; elevates the ribs
C. Spinous processes of T6 to T12; adducts the arm
D. Transverse processes of C1 to C4; elevates the scapula
E. Spinous processes of T11 to L2; depresses the ribs
Answer: A. Spinous processes of T2 to T5; retracts the scapula
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 5 marks rhomboid major, which arises from the spinous processes of T2 to T5 and attaches to the medial border of the scapula inferior to the spine.
Pulling the medial border toward the midline retracts the scapula.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. C7 to T1 is the origin of rhomboid minor, and neither rhomboid elevates the ribs.
C. The lower thoracic spinous processes and arm adduction describe latissimus dorsi.
D. C1 to C4 transverse processes and scapular elevation describe levator scapulae.
E. T11 to L2 and rib depression describe serratus posterior inferior.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1700
A 41-year-old man is evaluated after a deep laceration near the medial border of his left scapula. On examination, he has weakness when squeezing his shoulder blades together, and elevation of the left scapula is mildly weak even though shrugging is still possible. Injury to a single nerve is suspected. Which of the following muscles would also be weakened by injury to this nerve?
A. Trapezius
B. Levator scapulae
C. Latissimus dorsi
D. Serratus posterior superior
E. Splenius
Answer: B. Levator scapulae
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Weak scapular retraction with mild weakness of elevation fits injury to the dorsal scapular nerve, which supplies rhomboid major, rhomboid minor, and levator scapulae.
Levator scapulae elevates the scapula, so its loss weakens elevation, while trapezius (CN XI) keeps the shrug possible.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Trapezius is innervated by CN XI, which explains why he can still shrug.
C. Latissimus dorsi is innervated by the thoracodorsal nerve.
D. Serratus posterior superior is innervated by the 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves.
E. Splenius is a deep back muscle innervated by posterior rami.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1701
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Cadaveric dissection of the upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-12-rhomboids-both-sides.png)
A. Rhomboid major muscle
B. Serratus posterior superior muscle
C. Rhomboid minor muscle
D. Levator scapulae muscle
E. Semispinalis muscle
Answer: C. Rhomboid minor muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the narrow, thicker band just superior to rhomboid major, running from the C7 and T1 spinous processes to the medial edge of the scapula at the spine.
That position and shape identify rhomboid minor, though the separation between the two rhomboids isn't always distinct.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Rhomboid major is the wider, flatter muscle inferior to it (pin 5).
B. Serratus posterior superior is deep to the rhomboids and hidden in this view.
D. Levator scapulae runs superiorly from the superior angle toward the cervical transverse processes (pin 1).
E. Semispinalis is a deep muscle, covered here by the superficial layer.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1702
A researcher studies the muscle marked by pin 1 in the attached image, which lies just superior to the muscle marked by pin 4. Which of the following describes its attachments?
![Cadaveric dissection of the upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-13-rhomboids-cut.png)
A. Spinous processes of C7 and T1 to the medial edge of the scapula at the spine
B. Nuchal ligament and C7 to T6 spinous processes to the occipital bone
C. Nuchal ligament and C7 to T3 spinous processes to the superior borders of ribs 2 to 4
D. Spinous processes of T2 to T5 to the medial border of the scapula inferior to the spine
E. Transverse processes of C1 to C4 to the superior angle of the scapula
Answer: A. Spinous processes of C7 and T1 to the medial edge of the scapula at the spine
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks rhomboid minor, which runs from the spinous processes of C7 and T1 to the medial edge of the scapula at the level of the spine.
Rhomboid major (pin 4) sits below it and runs from T2 to T5 to the medial border below the spine.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. This describes splenius, a deep muscle of the neck.
C. This describes serratus posterior superior, which lies deep to the rhomboids.
D. This describes rhomboid major, the muscle at pin 4.
E. This describes levator scapulae (pin 2).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1703
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image, in which the rhomboids are being cut along their medial attachments. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Cadaveric dissection of the upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-13-rhomboids-cut.png)
A. Rhomboid minor muscle
B. Levator scapulae muscle
C. Trapezius muscle
D. Serratus posterior superior muscle
E. Splenius muscle
Answer: B. Levator scapulae muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the relatively thin, strap-like muscle that runs superiorly from the region of the superior angle of the scapula toward the cervical transverse processes.
This is levator scapulae, which lies deep to trapezius alongside the rhomboids.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Rhomboid minor is the muscle at pin 1, running horizontally toward the C7 and T1 spinous processes.
C. Trapezius has already been reflected in this dissection.
D. Serratus posterior superior lies deep to the rhomboids and isn't exposed yet.
E. Splenius is a deeper neck muscle running toward the skull and is medial to this field.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1704
A researcher studies the muscle marked by pin 1 in the attached image. Electrical stimulation of this muscle in a cadaver model moves the scapula. Which of the following best describes the movement produced?
![Cadaveric dissection of the right upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-14-dorsal-scapular-right.png)
A. Retraction of the scapula toward the midline
B. Elevation of the ribs during respiration
C. Elevation of the scapula with inferior rotation of the glenoid cavity
D. Medial rotation of the arm
E. Depression of the scapula
Answer: C. Elevation of the scapula with inferior rotation of the glenoid cavity
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks levator scapulae, which runs from the transverse processes of C1 to C4 to the superior angle and medial border of the scapula.
Pulling up on the superior angle elevates the scapula and rotates it so the glenoid cavity points inferiorly.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Retraction is the main action of the rhomboids and the middle part of trapezius.
B. Elevating the ribs is the proposed action of serratus posterior superior (pin 3).
D. Medial rotation of the arm is an action of latissimus dorsi.
E. Depression comes from trapezius, not a muscle that pulls upward on the superior angle.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1705
A first-year medical student examines the dissection of the upper back shown in the attached image. The rhomboids have been removed on the left side of the image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-back-serratus-post-sup.png)
A. Splenius muscle
B. Erector spinae muscles
C. Serratus posterior superior muscle
D. Serratus posterior inferior muscle
E. Rhomboid minor muscle
Answer: C. Serratus posterior superior muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Serratus posterior superior and inferior
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the thin, pale sheet running inferolaterally from the upper thoracic spinous processes toward the upper ribs, exposed once the rhomboids are removed.
That depth, location, and fiber direction identify serratus posterior superior, which runs from the nuchal ligament and C7 to T3 spinous processes to ribs 2 to 4.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Splenius is the neck muscle higher in the field (pin 1).
B. The erector spinae are deep to this sheet and run vertically.
D. Serratus posterior inferior is in the lower thoracic region, deep to latissimus dorsi.
E. Rhomboid minor has been removed here, which is what exposes this layer.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1706
A researcher studies the muscle marked by pin 3 in the attached image. Which of the following describes its distal attachment and innervation?
![Cadaveric dissection of the right upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-14-dorsal-scapular-right.png)
A. Medial border of the scapula; dorsal scapular nerve
B. Superior borders of ribs 2 to 4; dorsal scapular nerve
C. Transverse processes of C1 to C3; posterior rami of spinal nerves
D. Inferior borders of ribs 8 to 12; 9th to 12th intercostal nerves
E. Superior borders of ribs 2 to 4; 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves
Answer: E. Superior borders of ribs 2 to 4; 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Serratus posterior superior and inferior
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks serratus posterior superior, which runs from the nuchal ligament and C7 to T3 spinous processes to the superior borders of ribs 2 to 4.
It's innervated by the 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves (anterior rami of T2 to T5) and is thought to elevate the ribs and serve a proprioceptive role.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. This describes the rhomboids, which were reflected to expose this muscle.
B. The rib attachment is right, but the dorsal scapular nerve supplies the rhomboids and levator scapulae, not this muscle.
C. This describes splenius cervicis, a deep muscle.
D. This describes serratus posterior inferior.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1707
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image, in which latissimus dorsi has been reflected. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-09-latissimus-reflected.png)
A. Serratus posterior inferior muscle
B. Iliocostalis muscle
C. Serratus posterior superior muscle
D. Thoracolumbar fascia
E. Rhomboid major muscle
Answer: A. Serratus posterior inferior muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Serratus posterior superior and inferior
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks a very thin muscle in the lower thoracic region that becomes visible only after latissimus dorsi is reflected, which identifies serratus posterior inferior.
It's often adhered to the deep surface of latissimus dorsi and can be removed with it by accident.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Iliocostalis is part of the erector spinae, deep to the intermediate layer.
C. Serratus posterior superior lies in the upper thoracic region, deep to the rhomboids.
D. The thoracolumbar fascia is the aponeurotic sheet lower in the field (pin 4).
E. Rhomboid major is higher, between the upper thoracic spines and the scapula.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1708
A researcher studies the intermediate back muscle marked by pin 4 in the attached image. Which of the following best describes its attachments and innervation?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-16-intermediate-layer.png)
A. Iliac crest and sacrum to the angles of the ribs; posterior rami of spinal nerves
B. Nuchal ligament and C7 to T3 spinous processes to ribs 2 to 4; 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves
C. T11 to L2 spinous processes to ribs 8 to 12; 9th to 12th intercostal nerves
D. T11 to L2 spinous processes to ribs 8 to 12; thoracodorsal nerve
E. T2 to T5 spinous processes to the medial border of the scapula; dorsal scapular nerve
Answer: C. T11 to L2 spinous processes to ribs 8 to 12; 9th to 12th intercostal nerves
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Serratus posterior superior and inferior
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks serratus posterior inferior, which runs from the spinous processes of T11 to L2 to the inferior borders of ribs 8 to 12.
It's innervated by the 9th to 12th intercostal nerves (anterior rami of T9 to T12) and is thought to depress the ribs and serve a proprioceptive role.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. This describes iliocostalis, a deep back muscle.
B. This describes serratus posterior superior (pin 2).
D. The attachments are right, but the thoracodorsal nerve supplies latissimus dorsi, the muscle reflected off this one.
E. This describes rhomboid major.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1709
A first-year medical student examines the dissection of the posterior neck shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-17-splenius-semispinalis.png)
A. Semispinalis muscle
B. Levator scapulae muscle
C. Splenius muscle
D. Longissimus muscle
E. Trapezius muscle
Answer: C. Splenius muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Splenius muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the thick, flat muscle wrapping the posterolateral neck like a bandage, with fibers running superolaterally from the midline toward the skull and upper cervical vertebrae.
That shape and fiber direction identify splenius.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Semispinalis is the vertically oriented muscle deep and medial to splenius (pin 2).
B. Levator scapulae runs toward the scapula and is lateral to this dissection.
D. Longissimus is part of the erector spinae, deeper and lower in the back.
E. Trapezius has been removed to expose this layer.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1710
A 30-year-old woman is evaluated after a sudden neck injury. Testing of the muscle marked by pin 3 in the attached image on the right side alone is planned. When only the right-sided muscle contracts, which of the following movements is produced?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-17-splenius-semispinalis.png)
A. Lateral flexion of the neck to the right and rotation of the head to the right
B. Flexion of the neck
C. Extension of the head without rotation
D. Elevation of the right scapula
E. Lateral flexion of the neck to the right and rotation of the head to the left
Answer: A. Lateral flexion of the neck to the right and rotation of the head to the right
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Splenius muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks splenius, which acting unilaterally laterally flexes the neck and rotates the head to the same side as the active muscle.
Acting bilaterally, the two sides together extend the head and neck.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Splenius is a posterior muscle and extends rather than flexes the neck.
C. Pure extension comes from both sides acting together, not one side alone.
D. Scapular elevation is done by trapezius and levator scapulae.
E. Contralateral rotation is the action of semispinalis, not splenius.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1711
A researcher maps the innervation of the muscle marked by pin 2 in the attached image, which lies just deep to the muscle at pin 3. Which of the following nerves supplies it?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-17-splenius-semispinalis.png)
A. Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI)
B. Posterior rami of spinal nerves
C. Intercostal nerves
D. Dorsal scapular nerve
E. Anterior rami of C3 and C4
Answer: B. Posterior rami of spinal nerves
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Back muscle layers and their innervation
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks semispinalis, one of the deep (intrinsic) back muscles.
All of the deep back muscles, including splenius, semispinalis, and the erector spinae, are innervated by posterior rami of spinal nerves.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. CN XI supplies trapezius, a superficial extrinsic muscle.
C. Intercostal nerves supply the serratus posterior muscles of the intermediate layer.
D. The dorsal scapular nerve supplies levator scapulae and the rhomboids.
E. The anterior rami of C3 and C4 carry proprioception from trapezius and contribute to levator scapulae.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1712
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image, in which splenius has been reflected superiorly. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-18-semispinalis.png)
A. Spinalis muscle
B. Levator scapulae muscle
C. Nuchal ligament
D. Semispinalis muscle
E. Splenius muscle
Answer: D. Semispinalis muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Semispinalis muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the vertically oriented muscle exposed directly deep to the reflected splenius, which identifies semispinalis.
It runs alongside the midline toward the occipital bone between the superior and inferior nuchal lines.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Spinalis is the most medial column of the erector spinae in the thoracic region, below this field.
B. Levator scapulae is lateral and runs toward the scapula.
C. The nuchal ligament is the midline band marked by pin 2.
E. Splenius is the reflected muscle at pin 3.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1713
A 45-year-old man is evaluated for neck pain. Contraction of the right muscle marked by pin 1 in the attached image is tested in isolation. Which of the following movements does it produce?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-18-semispinalis.png)
A. Extension of the head and rotation to the left
B. Lateral flexion of the vertebral column only
C. Extension of the head and rotation to the right
D. Elevation of the ribs
E. Flexion of the head and rotation to the right
Answer: A. Extension of the head and rotation to the left
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Semispinalis muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks semispinalis, which extends and contralaterally rotates the head and vertebral column.
The right semispinalis therefore extends the head and turns it to the left.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Lateral flexion is the main unilateral action of the erector spinae.
C. Rotation to the same side is the action of splenius, not semispinalis.
D. Rib elevation is the proposed action of serratus posterior superior.
E. Semispinalis is posterior to the axis of motion, so it extends rather than flexes.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1714
A researcher studies the superior attachment of semispinalis on a dried skull. Which of the following best describes where it attaches?
A. Occipital bone between the superior and inferior nuchal lines
B. Superior nuchal line
C. Mastoid process
D. External occipital protuberance only
E. Occipital condyles
Answer: A. Occipital bone between the superior and inferior nuchal lines
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Semispinalis muscle
Why this is correct:
Semispinalis attaches superiorly to the occipital bone between the superior and inferior nuchal lines and inferiorly to the transverse processes of C4 to T10.
That high occipital attachment lets it extend the head.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The superior nuchal line receives splenius capitis.
C. The mastoid process receives longissimus.
D. The external occipital protuberance anchors the nuchal ligament and trapezius.
E. The occipital condyles articulate with the atlas and aren't a muscle attachment in this lecture.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1715
A first-year medical student examines the dissection of the deep back shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Cadaveric dissection of the deep back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-back-erector-spinae.png)
A. Iliocostalis muscle
B. Serratus posterior inferior muscle
C. Semispinalis muscle
D. Longissimus muscle
E. Spinalis muscle
Answer: A. Iliocostalis muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Erector spinae muscles
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the most lateral of the three vertical columns of the erector spinae, lying over the ribs, which is iliocostalis.
From lateral to medial the columns are iliocostalis, longissimus, and spinalis.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Serratus posterior inferior is a thin intermediate muscle superficial to the erector spinae and has been removed here.
C. Semispinalis is in the neck and upper thorax, deep to splenius.
D. Longissimus is the intermediate column (pin 3).
E. Spinalis is the most medial column (pin 4).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1716

B. Iliocostalis muscle

Pin 5 marks the intermediate column of the erector spinae, which sits between iliocostalis laterally and spinalis medially and is usually the widest of the three.
That position identifies longissimus.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Splenius is in the neck, at the top of the field (pin 1).
B. Iliocostalis is the most lateral column (pin 6).
D. Spinalis is the most medial column (pin 4).
E. Latissimus dorsi has been reflected laterally (pin 7).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1717
A first-year medical student examines the dissection of the deep back shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?

Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1718

Pin 5 marks longissimus, the intermediate column of the erector spinae.

Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The superior nuchal line receives splenius capitis.

Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1719
A 38-year-old man comes to the clinic because of low back pain that began the day after he lifted a heavy couch while twisting. Examination shows tenderness and tightness of the paraspinal muscles on both sides of the lumbar spine, with no numbness or weakness in the legs. A back strain is suspected. Which of the following best describes the innervation and bilateral action of the muscle group most often injured in this condition?
A. Intercostal nerves; elevation and depression of the ribs
B. Anterior rami of spinal nerves; flexion of the vertebral column
C. Posterior rami of spinal nerves; extension of the vertebral column and head
D. Posterior rami of spinal nerves; contralateral rotation of the vertebral column
E. Thoracodorsal nerve; extension and adduction of the arm
Answer: C. Posterior rami of spinal nerves; extension of the vertebral column and head
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Back muscle layers and their innervation
Why this is correct:
A back strain is stretching or microscopic tearing of muscle fibers, most often in the erector spinae, and it's the most common cause of low back pain.
The erector spinae are deep back muscles innervated by posterior rami; acting bilaterally they extend the vertebral column and head, and acting unilaterally they laterally flex the vertebral column.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Intercostal nerves and rib movement describe the serratus posterior muscles.
B. The deep back muscles aren't supplied by anterior rami, and they extend rather than flex the vertebral column.
D. The innervation is right, but contralateral rotation is the action of semispinalis, not the erector spinae.
E. The thoracodorsal nerve and arm movement describe latissimus dorsi.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1720
A researcher stimulates the muscles marked by pins 4, 5, and 6 in the attached image on the right side only. Which of the following movements is produced?
![Cadaveric dissection of the deep back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-19-erector-spinae.png)
A. Retraction of the right scapula
B. Lateral flexion of the vertebral column to the left
C. Lateral flexion of the vertebral column to the right
D. Rotation of the head to the left
E. Extension of the vertebral column with no lateral movement
Answer: C. Lateral flexion of the vertebral column to the right
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Erector spinae muscles
Why this is correct:
Pins 4, 5, and 6 mark spinalis, longissimus, and iliocostalis, the three columns of the erector spinae.
Acting unilaterally, the erector spinae laterally flex the vertebral column toward the same side, so the right group bends the column to the right.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Scapular retraction is done by trapezius and the rhomboids, not the erector spinae.
B. Each side flexes the column toward itself, not away from itself.
D. Rotation of the head to the opposite side is an action of semispinalis.
E. Pure extension happens when both sides contract together.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1721
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image, in which trapezius has been reflected to show its deep surface. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Cadaveric dissection of the deep surface of trapezius](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-05-trapezius-deep-nv-bundle.png)
A. Transverse cervical artery
B. Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI)
C. Thoracodorsal nerve
D. Dorsal scapular nerve
E. Posterior ramus of a cervical spinal nerve
Answer: B. Spinal accessory nerve (CN XI)
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Trapezius muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the nerve running along the deep surface of the reflected trapezius, which is the spinal accessory nerve, the motor supply to trapezius.
It travels with the transverse cervical vessels in the neurovascular bundle on the muscle's deep side.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The transverse cervical artery travels with this nerve but is the vessel marked by pin 2.
C. The thoracodorsal nerve runs on the deep surface of latissimus dorsi.
D. The dorsal scapular nerve runs deep to levator scapulae and the rhomboids, not on the deep surface of trapezius.
E. Posterior rami supply the deep back muscles and don't run on the underside of trapezius.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1722
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Cadaveric dissection of the deep surface of trapezius](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-05-trapezius-deep-nv-bundle.png)
A. Posterior intercostal artery and vein
B. Transverse cervical artery and vein
C. Thoracodorsal artery and vein
D. Dorsal scapular artery and vein
E. Vertebral artery and vein
Answer: B. Transverse cervical artery and vein
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Trapezius muscle; Arterial supply of the vertebral column
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the vessels running with the spinal accessory nerve on the deep surface of trapezius, which are the transverse cervical artery and vein.
The transverse cervical artery is the blood supply to trapezius.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Posterior intercostal arteries run with the ribs and give spinal branches to the vertebral column.
C. The thoracodorsal vessels supply latissimus dorsi and run on its deep surface.
D. The dorsal scapular vessels supply the rhomboids and levator scapulae and run deep to those muscles.
E. The vertebral artery runs through the transverse foramina of the cervical vertebrae, not on trapezius.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1723

B. Serratus posterior inferior

D. Trapezius
E. Rhomboid major
Answer: D. Trapezius

Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Trapezius muscle; Back muscle layers and their innervation
Why this is correct:

Why the other answers are wrong:

Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1727

E. Dorsal scapular nerve and vessels
Answer: E. Dorsal scapular nerve and vessels

Difficulty: Hard

Why the other answers are wrong:

B. CN XI and the transverse cervical vessels run on the deep surface of trapezius, a more superficial layer.

Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1728

The dorsal scapular nerve innervates rhomboid major, rhomboid minor, and levator scapulae, and the dorsal scapular artery supplies the same three muscles.
Why the other answers are wrong:

Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1729

Question 1730
A first-year medical student examines the dissection of the posterior neck shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-17-splenius-semispinalis.png)
A. Posterior longitudinal ligament
B. Ligamentum flavum
C. Nuchal ligament
D. Semispinalis muscle
E. Supraspinous ligament
Answer: C. Nuchal ligament
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Supraspinous, interspinous, and nuchal ligaments
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the strong midline band of the posterior neck that runs from the external occipital protuberance to the C7 spinous process, which is the nuchal ligament.
It serves as an attachment site for muscles such as trapezius, splenius, and serratus posterior superior.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The posterior longitudinal ligament lies within the vertebral canal on the backs of the vertebral bodies.
B. Ligamentum flavum joins adjacent laminae inside the vertebral canal region and isn't visible in this superficial view.
D. Semispinalis is the vertically oriented muscle marked by pin 2.
E. The supraspinous ligament runs along the tips of the spinous processes from C7 down to the sacrum; above C7 it merges with the nuchal ligament.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1731
A 27-year-old woman is evaluated after a fall in which her head was forced sharply forward. The structure marked by pin 4 in the attached image is thought to have been stretched. Which of the following best describes the normal function of this structure?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-17-splenius-semispinalis.png)
A. Joins adjacent laminae within the vertebral canal
B. Limits forward flexion of the head and cervical spine
C. Prevents hyperextension of the neck
D. Connects adjacent transverse processes
E. Resists posterior displacement of the intervertebral discs
Answer: B. Limits forward flexion of the head and cervical spine
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Supraspinous, interspinous, and nuchal ligaments
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the nuchal ligament, which spans from the external occipital protuberance to the C7 spinous process.
It limits forward flexion of the head and cervical spine and serves as an attachment site for muscles.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Joining adjacent laminae is the ligamentum flavum.
C. Preventing hyperextension is the role of the anterior longitudinal ligament.
D. Connecting transverse processes is the intertransverse ligament.
E. Resisting posterior disc displacement is the role of the posterior longitudinal ligament.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1732

Question 1733
