Question 1735
A first-year medical student examines the posterior surface of the right scapula shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Photograph of a scapula, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-scapula-posterior.png)
A. Superior angle of the scapula
B. Coracoid process
C. Acromion process of the scapula
D. Inferior angle of the scapula
E. Spine of the scapula
Answer: A. Superior angle of the scapula
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the upper medial corner of the scapula, where the medial and superior borders meet, which is the superior angle.
Levator scapulae runs from the transverse processes of C1 to C4 down to the superior angle and medial border.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The coracoid process projects anteriorly from the lateral part of the superior border and isn't a structure on your lab list.
C. The acromion is the lateral end of the spine at the tip of the shoulder (pin 3).
D. The inferior angle is the lowest point of the scapula (pin 7).
E. The spine of the scapula is the ridge running across the posterior surface (pin 4).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1736
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image. Which of the following muscles attaches to the bony edge marked by pin 5 inferior to the spine?
![Cadaveric dissection of the upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-13-rhomboids-cut.png)
A. Levator scapulae
B. Trapezius
C. Latissimus dorsi
D. Rhomboid minor
E. Rhomboid major
Answer: E. Rhomboid major
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 5 marks the medial (vertebral) border of the scapula.
Rhomboid major attaches to the medial border between the spine and the inferior angle, while rhomboid minor attaches at the level of the spine and levator scapulae attaches above it.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Levator scapulae attaches to the superior angle and the medial border above the spine.
B. Trapezius attaches to the spine and acromion, not the medial border.
C. Latissimus dorsi attaches to the humerus, not the scapula.
D. Rhomboid minor attaches at the medial edge of the scapular spine, not below it.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1737
A first-year medical student examines the dissection shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-06-trapezius-reflected.png)
A. Thoracolumbar fascia
B. Supraspinous ligament
C. Erector spinae tendon
D. Serratus posterior inferior muscle
E. Latissimus dorsi muscle
Answer: A. Thoracolumbar fascia
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Latissimus dorsi muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the broad, shiny aponeurotic sheet over the lower back, which is the thoracolumbar fascia.
Latissimus dorsi arises in part from this fascia.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The supraspinous ligament is a narrow cord along the tips of the spinous processes.
C. The erector spinae tendon lies deep to the fascia and is only seen after the fascia is opened.
D. Serratus posterior inferior is deep to latissimus dorsi and isn't exposed in this view.
E. Latissimus dorsi is the muscle belly superolateral to the fascia (pin 2).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1738
A researcher studies the skull attachments of the deep back muscles of the neck. Splenius capitis is traced to its superior attachment. Which of the following is the attachment site?
A. Mastoid process
B. External occipital protuberance
C. Occipital bone between the superior and inferior nuchal lines
D. Inferior nuchal line
E. Superior nuchal line
Answer: E. Superior nuchal line
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Semispinalis muscle; Supraspinous, interspinous, and nuchal ligaments
Why this is correct:
Splenius capitis attaches superiorly to the occipital bone at the superior nuchal line, while splenius cervicis attaches to the transverse processes of C1 to C3 or C4.
Both parts arise from the nuchal ligament and the C7 to T6 spinous processes.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The mastoid process receives longissimus.
B. The external occipital protuberance anchors the nuchal ligament.
C. The area between the nuchal lines receives semispinalis.
D. The inferior nuchal line is the lower boundary of the semispinalis attachment.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1739
A 25-year-old rock climber comes to the clinic because of pain at his lower back where a large back muscle arises. He has pain when pulling his body upward on a climbing wall. Which of the following groups of bony and fascial sites gives origin to the muscle most likely involved?
A. Occipital bone, nuchal ligament, and C7 to T12 spinous processes
B. Lower thoracic spinous processes, thoracolumbar fascia, and iliac crest
C. Transverse processes of C1 to C4
D. Iliac crest, posterior sacrum, and sacral and inferior lumbar spinous processes through a broad tendon
E. Spinous processes of T11 to L2 only
Answer: B. Lower thoracic spinous processes, thoracolumbar fascia, and iliac crest
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Latissimus dorsi muscle
Why this is correct:
Pulling the body up a wall uses adduction and extension of the arm, the actions of latissimus dorsi, which is known as the climber's muscle.
Latissimus dorsi arises from the lower thoracic spinous processes, the thoracolumbar fascia, and the iliac crest (with the inferior ribs) and inserts in the intertubercular groove.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. These are the proximal attachments of trapezius.
C. These are the proximal attachments of levator scapulae.
D. This broad tendon is the origin of the erector spinae.
E. T11 to L2 is the origin of serratus posterior inferior.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1740
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image, seen from above and from the side. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 6?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Intervertebral disc
B. Lamina
C. Vertebral foramen
D. Pedicle
E. Vertebral body
Answer: E. Vertebral body
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 6 marks the large, rounded anterior part of the vertebra, which is the vertebral body.
It supports body weight and is separated from the bodies above and below by intervertebral discs.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The disc sits between two bodies and isn't part of a single vertebra.
B. The laminae are the flat plates that close the vertebral arch posteriorly.
C. The vertebral foramen is the opening between the body and the arch.
D. The pedicles are the short processes joining the arch to the body.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1741
A 71-year-old woman comes to the clinic because her family has noticed she is becoming hunched forward. She has had two low-impact fractures in the past and has never taken treatment for bone loss. Erosion and collapse of the part of several thoracic vertebrae marked by pin 6 in the attached image is suspected. Which of the following abnormal curvatures is most likely developing?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Spondylolisthesis
B. Loss of the normal sacral curvature
C. Lordosis
D. Kyphosis
E. Scoliosis
Answer: D. Kyphosis
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Abnormal vertebral curvatures: kyphosis, lordosis, and scoliosis
Why this is correct:
Pin 6 marks the vertebral body, and osteoporosis most commonly weakens thoracic vertebral bodies, especially in postmenopausal women.
Erosion or fracture of the anterior part of thoracic vertebrae produces kyphosis, an exaggerated thoracic curvature (hunchback).
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Spondylolisthesis is slippage of one vertebra on another, most often L5 on S1.
B. The sacral curvature isn't affected by thoracic body collapse.
C. Lordosis is an exaggerated lumbar curvature linked to weak trunk muscles, obesity, and pregnancy.
E. Scoliosis is a lateral curvature with vertebral rotation, usually idiopathic or congenital.
Objective: Evaluate normal vertebral curvatures, and distinguish features of abnormal vertebral curvatures such as kyphosis, lordosis, and scoliosis.

Question 1742
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Vertebral foramen
B. Superior vertebral notch
C. Intervertebral foramen
D. Transverse foramen
E. Vertebral body
Answer: A. Vertebral foramen
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the central opening bounded by the back of the vertebral body and the vertebral arch, which is the vertebral foramen.
When vertebrae are stacked, the foramina line up to form the vertebral canal, which holds the spinal cord and meninges.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The superior vertebral notch is a groove on the upper edge of the pedicle, seen from the side.
C. The intervertebral foramen is formed between two adjacent vertebrae by their notches and transmits spinal nerves.
D. Transverse foramina are small openings in the transverse processes, found only in cervical vertebrae.
E. The vertebral body is the solid anterior part (pin 6).
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1743
A researcher compares dried vertebrae from different regions. One has a small, round opening at the location marked by pin 2 in the attached image, while another has a large, triangular opening at the same location. Which of the following correctly matches these vertebrae to their regions?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Small round opening: sacral; large triangular opening: thoracic
B. Small round opening: cervical; large triangular opening: sacral
C. Small round opening: thoracic; large triangular opening: cervical
D. Small round opening: cervical; large triangular opening: thoracic
E. Small round opening: lumbar; large triangular opening: thoracic
Answer: C. Small round opening: thoracic; large triangular opening: cervical
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Comparing cervical, thoracic, and lumbar vertebrae
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the vertebral foramen. Thoracic vertebrae have a small, round vertebral foramen.
Cervical vertebrae (especially C3 to C7) have a large, triangular foramen to accommodate the cervical enlargement of the spinal cord, and lumbar foramina are triangular and larger than thoracic ones.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The sacrum's canal isn't described this way, and thoracic foramina are round rather than triangular.
B. Cervical foramina are triangular, not small and round.
D. This reverses the two regions.
E. Lumbar foramina are triangular, not small and round.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1744
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 10 in the lateral view?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Lamina
B. Spinous process
C. Pedicle
D. Transverse process
E. Inferior articular process
Answer: B. Spinous process
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 10 marks the process projecting posteriorly from the vertebral arch at the junction of the two laminae, which is the spinous process.
It's an attachment site for muscles and ligaments, including the supraspinous and interspinous ligaments.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The laminae are the flat plates between the transverse and spinous processes.
C. The pedicle joins the arch to the body (pin 8).
D. Transverse processes project posterolaterally from the junction of the pedicle and lamina (pins 4 and 7).
E. The inferior articular process projects downward to meet the vertebra below.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1745
A physician palpates the back of a seated patient whose neck is flexed, as shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Photograph of the back of the neck and upper back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-22-vertebra-prominens.png)
A. External occipital protuberance
B. Spinous process of T12
C. Spinous process of C7
D. Spinous process of C1
E. Spinous process of C2
Answer: C. Spinous process of C7
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Cervical vertebrae (C3 to C7) and the vertebra prominens
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the prominent bump at the base of the flexed neck, which is the spinous process of C7.
C7 is called the vertebra prominens because its long spinous process is easily felt and serves as a surface landmark.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The external occipital protuberance is on the skull, at the top of the neck.
B. T12 is much lower, at the level of the lower ribs.
D. C1 (the atlas) has no spinous process.
E. C2's spinous process is high in the neck and buried under muscle.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1746
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Transverse process
B. Lamina
C. Pedicle
D. Superior articular process
E. Spinous process
Answer: B. Lamina
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks one of the two broad, flat plates that close the vertebral arch posteriorly, between the transverse and spinous processes.
These plates are the laminae.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The transverse process projects laterally (pin 4).
C. The pedicle is the short, stout process joining the arch to the vertebral body (pin 5).
D. The superior articular process projects upward from the junction of the pedicle and lamina.
E. The spinous process projects posteriorly from where the two laminae meet (pin 1).
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1747
A 63-year-old man has progressive leg pain and numbness from compression of lumbar nerve roots by a tumor in the vertebral canal. A surgeon plans to remove the spinous processes and the structure marked by pin 3 in the attached image at the affected levels. Which of the following best describes this procedure and its purpose?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Discectomy, to remove the nucleus pulposus pressing on the nerve root
B. Laminectomy, to open the vertebral canal and relieve pressure on the spinal cord or nerve roots
C. Laminectomy, to fuse the adjacent vertebral bodies and stop vertebral slippage
D. Pediculectomy, to widen the intervertebral foramen for the exiting spinal nerve
E. Facetectomy, to remove the zygapophysial joint and restore rotation
Answer: B. Laminectomy, to open the vertebral canal and relieve pressure on the spinal cord or nerve roots
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Vertebral canal; Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the lamina. Surgical removal of one or more spinous processes with their supporting laminae is a laminectomy.
It gives access to the vertebral canal to relieve pressure on the spinal cord or nerve roots from a tumor, herniated disc, or bony overgrowth.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. A discectomy removes disc material, not the posterior arch.
C. The name is right, but its purpose is decompression, not fusion of vertebral bodies.
D. The procedure removes the laminae, not the pedicles.
E. The facet joints aren't the structures being removed.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1748
A newborn has a tuft of hair over the lower back. Imaging shows that the structures marked by pin 3 in the attached image failed to fuse at one lumbar level, but the meninges and spinal cord haven't herniated. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Spondylolisthesis
B. Spondylolysis
C. Myelomeningocele
D. Meningocele
E. Spina bifida occulta
Answer: E. Spina bifida occulta
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra; Vertebral canal
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the laminae. Spina bifida is failure of the laminae of the vertebral arch to develop and fuse, usually in the lumbar region.
In spina bifida occulta, skin (often with a tuft of hair) covers the defect and nothing herniates through it.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Spondylolisthesis is displacement of one vertebra relative to another.
B. Spondylolysis is a defect or fracture of the pars interarticularis.
C. A myelomeningocele has both the meninges and spinal cord herniating through the defect.
D. A meningocele has the meninges herniating through the defect.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1749
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 5?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Transverse process
B. Pedicle
C. Lamina
D. Superior articular process
E. Vertebral body
Answer: B. Pedicle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 5 marks the short, stout process joining the vertebral arch to the vertebral body, which is the pedicle.
Each pedicle carries a superior and an inferior vertebral notch.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The transverse process projects laterally (pin 4).
C. The lamina is the flat plate closing the arch posteriorly (pin 3).
D. The superior articular process projects upward to meet the vertebra above.
E. The vertebral body is the large anterior part (pin 6).
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1750
A 17-year-old gymnast comes to the clinic because of low back pain. An oblique radiograph of the lumbar spine shows the "Scotty dog" outline. Which of the following parts of the vertebra forms the eye of the Scotty dog?
A. Pedicle
B. Pars interarticularis
C. Superior articular process
D. Transverse process
E. Inferior articular process
Answer: A. Pedicle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
On an oblique lumbar radiograph, the pedicle forms the eye of the Scotty dog.
The nose is the transverse process, the neck is the pars interarticularis, the ear is the superior articular process, and the front leg is the inferior articular process.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The pars interarticularis forms the neck.
C. The superior articular process forms the ear.
D. The transverse process forms the nose.
E. The inferior articular process forms the front leg.
Objective: Discuss the clinical presentation and anatomical basis for the following: spondylosis, spondylolisthesis and injuries associated with the vertebral column.

Question 1751
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Pedicle
B. Spinous process
C. Lamina
D. Transverse process
E. Superior articular process
Answer: D. Transverse process
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the process projecting posterolaterally from the junction of the pedicle and lamina, which is the transverse process.
Like the spinous process, it's an attachment site for muscles and ligaments.
Why the other answers are wrong:

B. The spinous process projects straight back in the midline (pin 1).
C. The lamina closes the arch posteriorly (pin 3).
E. The superior articular process projects upward and carries a facet for the vertebra above.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1752
A researcher studies muscles that arise from the structure marked by pin 4 in the attached image at different levels of the spine. Which of the following muscles arises from this structure on C1 to C4?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-12-typical-vertebra.png)
A. Splenius
B. Rhomboid minor
C. Trapezius
D. Levator scapulae
E. Serratus posterior superior
Answer: D. Levator scapulae
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Parts of a typical vertebra
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the transverse process. Levator scapulae arises from the transverse processes of C1 to C4 and inserts on the superior angle and medial border of the scapula.
Semispinalis also arises from transverse processes, but lower, from C4 to T10.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Splenius arises from the nuchal ligament and C7 to T6 spinous processes (splenius cervicis inserts on upper cervical transverse processes rather than arising from them).
B. Rhomboid minor arises from the spinous processes of C7 and T1.
C. Trapezius arises from the occipital bone, nuchal ligament, and spinous processes.
E. Serratus posterior superior arises from the nuchal ligament and spinous processes of C7 to T3.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1753
A first-year medical student reviews the lateral view of the vertebra in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?

B. Inferior articular process and facet

E. Superior articular process and facet
Answer: E. Superior articular process and facet

Difficulty: Medium

Pin 3 marks the process projecting upward from the junction of the pedicle and lamina, carrying a facet that articulates with the vertebra above.
That's the superior articular process and its superior articular facet.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The pedicle connects the arch to the body.
B. The inferior articular process projects downward to meet the vertebra below (pin 5).
C. The pars interarticularis is the region between the superior and inferior articular processes (pin 4).
D. The transverse process projects laterally and doesn't carry an articular facet for the vertebra above.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1754
A first-year medical student reviews the lateral view of the vertebra in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 5?

That's the inferior articular process and its inferior articular facet.
Why the other answers are wrong:

Question 1755
A first-year medical student reviews the superior view of the vertebra in the attached image. The blue surface marked by pin 2 faces upward. Which of the following structures does this surface articulate with?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-13-articular-processes.png)
A. The intervertebral disc
B. The head of a rib
C. The tubercle of a rib

E. The inferior articular facet of the vertebra above
Answer: E. The inferior articular facet of the vertebra above

Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Articular processes and pars interarticularis
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the superior articular facet on the superior articular process.
It articulates with the inferior articular facet of the vertebra above to form a zygapophysial (facet) joint.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Intervertebral discs join vertebral bodies, not articular processes.
B. Rib heads articulate with costal facets on thoracic vertebral bodies.
C. Rib tubercles articulate with transverse costal facets on thoracic transverse processes.
D. The inferior articular facet, not the superior one, meets the vertebra below.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1756
A first-year medical student reviews the superior view of the vertebra in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-13-articular-processes.png)
A. Inferior articular facet
B. Transverse costal facet
C. Superior articular facet
D. Mammillary process
E. Lamina
Answer: A. Inferior articular facet
Order: First
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Articular processes and pars interarticularis
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the articular surface on the downward-projecting inferior articular process, seen from above just beside the spinous process.
That surface is the inferior articular facet, which meets the superior articular facet of the vertebra below.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Transverse costal facets are found on thoracic transverse processes.
C. The superior articular facet faces upward on the superior articular process (pin 2).
D. The mammillary process is a lumbar feature on the superior articular process.
E. The lamina is the plate of bone, not an articular surface.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1757
A researcher studies the joint enclosed by the structure marked by pin 2 in the attached image. Which of the following best describes this joint?
![Illustration of the vertebral column, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-39-facet-joint.png)
A. Plane synovial joint innervated by branches of adjacent posterior rami
B. Plane synovial joint innervated by the intercostal nerves
C. Symphysis (secondary cartilaginous joint) innervated by meningeal branches
D. Fibrous joint with no innervation
E. Pivot synovial joint innervated by anterior rami
Answer: A. Plane synovial joint innervated by branches of adjacent posterior rami
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Facet (zygapophysial) joints
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the joint capsule between an inferior articular process (pin 1) and the superior articular process of the vertebra below (pin 3).
This is a zygapophysial (facet) joint, a plane synovial joint innervated by branches of adjacent posterior rami, and it's sensitive to pain.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The joint type is right, but the innervation comes from posterior rami, not intercostal nerves.
C. Intervertebral discs form symphyses between vertebral bodies, not between articular processes.
D. Facet joints have a capsule and synovial cavity and are innervated.
E. The pivot joint is between the dens and the atlas, not between articular processes.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1758
A first-year medical student reviews the lateral view of a lumbar vertebra in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Illustration of lumbar vertebrae, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-15-vertebral-notches.png)
A. Transverse foramen
B. Superior vertebral notch
C. Pars interarticularis
D. Inferior vertebral notch
E. Intervertebral foramen
Answer: B. Superior vertebral notch
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Vertebral notches and the intervertebral foramen
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the shallow groove on the upper edge of the pedicle, which is the superior vertebral notch.
It combines with the inferior vertebral notch of the vertebra above to form the intervertebral foramen.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Transverse foramina are found only in cervical vertebrae.
C. The pars interarticularis is between the articular processes, behind the pedicle.
D. The inferior vertebral notch is the deeper groove on the lower edge of the pedicle (pin 2).
E. The intervertebral foramen needs two vertebrae and is outlined in the right-hand figure.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1759
A first-year medical student reviews the lateral view of a lumbar vertebra in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Illustration of lumbar vertebrae, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-15-vertebral-notches.png)
A. Inferior articular facet
B. Inferior vertebral notch
C. Pedicle
D. Superior vertebral notch
E. Mammillary process
Answer: B. Inferior vertebral notch
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Vertebral notches and the intervertebral foramen
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the deep groove on the lower edge of the pedicle, which is the inferior vertebral notch.
With the superior vertebral notch of the vertebra below, it forms the intervertebral foramen.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The inferior articular facet is on the inferior articular process, posterior to this notch.
C. The pedicle is the bar of bone that carries both notches, not the notch itself.
D. The superior vertebral notch is the shallower groove on the upper edge of the pedicle (pin 1).
E. The mammillary process is on the superior articular process.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1760
A first-year medical student reviews the two articulated lumbar vertebrae in the right-hand figure of the attached image. Which of the following structures is outlined by the yellow dashed line?
![Illustration of lumbar vertebrae, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-15-vertebral-notches.png)
A. Intervertebral foramen
B. Transverse foramen
C. Anterior sacral foramen
D. Zygapophysial joint
E. Vertebral foramen
Answer: A. Intervertebral foramen
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Vertebral notches and the intervertebral foramen
Why this is correct:
The yellow outline marks the opening formed between two adjacent vertebrae by the inferior vertebral notch above and the superior vertebral notch below.
That opening is the intervertebral foramen, which lets the spinal nerve pass out of the vertebral canal.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Transverse foramina are in the transverse processes of cervical vertebrae.
C. Anterior sacral foramina are on the pelvic surface of the sacrum.
D. The zygapophysial joint is posterior to the foramen, between articular processes.
E. The vertebral foramen is the central opening of a single vertebra, holding the spinal cord.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1761
A 68-year-old man comes to the clinic because of pain and numbness in his legs. Imaging shows bulging intervertebral discs and bone spurs narrowing the openings outlined in yellow in the attached image at several lumbar levels. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
![Illustration of lumbar vertebrae, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-15-vertebral-notches.png)
A. Spinal stenosis
B. Spondylolisthesis
C. Spina bifida occulta
D. Whiplash injury
E. Spondylolysis
Answer: A. Spinal stenosis
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Spinal stenosis of the vertebral canal
Why this is correct:
The yellow outline marks the intervertebral foramen, and narrowing of the vertebral canal or intervertebral foramina is spinal stenosis.
It's common in the lumbar and cervical regions, often degenerative in people over 60, and caused by bulging discs and bone spurs that compress the spinal cord or nerve roots.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Spondylolisthesis is slippage of one vertebra on another.
C. Spina bifida occulta is a congenital failure of the laminae to fuse.
D. Whiplash is a hyperextension injury of the neck that tears the anterior longitudinal ligament.
E. Spondylolysis is a defect in the pars interarticularis.
Objective: Discuss the clinical presentation and anatomical basis for the following: spondylosis, spondylolisthesis and injuries associated with the vertebral column.

Question 1762
A first-year medical student reviews the lateral view of the vertebra in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-13-articular-processes.png)
A. Lamina
B. Pars interarticularis
C. Pedicle
D. Inferior vertebral notch
E. Transverse process
Answer: B. Pars interarticularis
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Articular processes and pars interarticularis; Spondylolysis and the Scotty dog sign
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the narrow region of bone between the superior and inferior articular processes, which is the pars interarticularis.
A fracture here is called spondylolysis.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The lamina is the plate closing the arch posteriorly, medial to the articular processes.
C. The pedicle connects the arch to the vertebral body, anterior to this region.
D. The inferior vertebral notch is on the lower edge of the pedicle.
E. The transverse process projects laterally.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1763
A 16-year-old gymnast comes to the clinic because of low back pain that worsens when she arches backward. An oblique radiograph of the lumbar spine shows what looks like a collar around the neck of the Scotty dog at L5. A stress fracture of the structure marked by pin 4 in the attached image is suspected. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-13-articular-processes.png)
A. Spinal stenosis
B. Herniated nucleus pulposus
C. Spondylolisthesis
D. Kyphosis
E. Spondylolysis
Answer: E. Spondylolysis
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Spondylolysis and the Scotty dog sign
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the pars interarticularis, which forms the neck of the Scotty dog on an oblique radiograph.
A defect or fracture through it creates the collar appearance and is called spondylolysis, a stress fracture common in young athletes who repeatedly hyperextend, usually at L5 or L4.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Spinal stenosis is narrowing of the canal or foramina, usually in older adults.
B. Disc herniation doesn't break the pars interarticularis.
C. Spondylolisthesis is forward displacement of one vertebra on another, which can follow spondylolysis but isn't the fracture itself.
D. Kyphosis is an exaggerated thoracic curve.
Objective: Discuss the clinical presentation and anatomical basis for the following: spondylosis, spondylolisthesis and injuries associated with the vertebral column.

Question 1764
A first-year medical student is handed the vertebra shown in the attached image during a bone practical. It has a small body, a short bifid spinous process, and openings in both transverse processes. From which region of the vertebral column does it most likely come?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-17-cervical.png)
A. Lumbar
B. Sacral
C. Thoracic
D. Coccygeal
E. Cervical
Answer: E. Cervical
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Cervical vertebrae (C3 to C7) and the vertebra prominens
Why this is correct:
A small body, short bifid spinous process, and transverse foramina are the distinguishing features of a typical cervical vertebra.
Transverse foramina are found in all cervical vertebrae and no others.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Lumbar vertebrae have large bodies and short, stout spinous processes, with no transverse foramina.
B. Sacral vertebrae are fused into the sacrum.
C. Thoracic vertebrae have heart-shaped bodies, costal facets, and long spinous processes that slope downward.
D. Coccygeal vertebrae are small, rudimentary, and fused.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1765
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-17-cervical.png)
A. Groove for the vertebral artery
B. Superior vertebral notch
C. Vertebral foramen
D. Intervertebral foramen
E. Transverse foramen
Answer: E. Transverse foramen
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Vertebral artery and the transverse foramina
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the opening in the transverse process of a cervical vertebra, which is the transverse foramen.
The vertebral artery usually enters these foramina at C6 and ascends through C1.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The groove for the vertebral artery is on the posterior arch of the atlas (C1).
B. The superior vertebral notch is a groove on the pedicle seen from the side.
C. The vertebral foramen is the large central opening for the spinal cord.
D. The intervertebral foramen is between two vertebrae, not within one.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1766
A 49-year-old man is evaluated after a neck injury. Imaging shows fractures through the openings marked by pin 2 in the attached image from C3 to C6, raising concern for injury to the artery that passes through them. Which of the following best describes the course and territory of this artery?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-17-cervical.png)
A. Enters at C6, ascends through C1, and supplies trapezius
B. Enters at C6, ascends through C1, passes through the foramen magnum, and contributes to the posterior circulation of the brain
C. Enters at C7, ascends to C2, and supplies the anterior circulation of the brain
D. Enters at C4, ascends through C1, and drains into the internal vertebral venous plexus
E. Enters at C1, descends to C6, and supplies the cervical vertebral bodies only
Answer: B. Enters at C6, ascends through C1, passes through the foramen magnum, and contributes to the posterior circulation of the brain
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Vertebral artery and the transverse foramina
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the transverse foramen, which transmits the vertebral artery.
The vertebral artery typically enters the transverse foramina at C6, ascends through C1, and passes through the foramen magnum to contribute to the posterior circulation of the brain.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The course is right, but trapezius is supplied by the transverse cervical artery.
C. The vertebral artery typically enters at C6, and it supplies the posterior, not anterior, circulation.
D. Arteries don't drain into venous plexuses; the vertebral vein passes through the C1 to C7 transverse foramina.
E. It ascends, not descends, and its main territory is the brain.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1767
A first-year medical student is shown the vertebra in the upper figure of the attached image, which has no body and no spinous process. Which of the following vertebrae is it?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior view, and the base of the skull, inferior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-20-atlas.png)
A. A typical lumbar vertebra
B. Axis (C2)
C. Vertebra prominens (C7)
D. Atlas (C1)
E. A typical thoracic vertebra
Answer: D. Atlas (C1)
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Atlas (C1)
Why this is correct:
The atlas is ring-shaped, with two lateral masses joined by an anterior arch (replacing the body) and a posterior arch, and it has no spinous process.
Its superior articular facets articulate with the occipital condyles of the skull.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Lumbar vertebrae have large bodies and short, stout spinous processes.
B. The axis has a body and a dens projecting upward from it.
C. C7 has a body and a long, prominent spinous process.
E. Thoracic vertebrae have heart-shaped bodies and long spinous processes.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1768
A first-year medical student reviews the vertebra shown in the attached image, which has no body and no spinous process. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?

E. Superior articular facet

Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Atlas (C1)
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the groove on the superior surface of the posterior arch of the atlas, which carries the vertebral artery.
After passing through the transverse foramina of C6 through C1, the vertebral artery runs in this groove before passing through the foramen magnum.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The facet for the dens is on the posterior surface of the anterior arch, at the opposite side of the ring.
C. The lateral mass is the thick block of bone bearing the articular facets.
D. The transverse foramen is the opening in the transverse process (pin 4).
E. The superior articular facet is the large concave surface on the lateral mass (pin 2).

Question 1769
A researcher studies the joint formed between the surface marked by pin 2 and the structures marked by pin 6 in the attached image. Which of the following movements is this joint mainly responsible for?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior view, and the base of the skull, inferior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-20-atlas.png)
A. Nodding of the head, with some side bending and limited rotation
B. Flexion and extension of the lumbar spine
C. Pivoting of the head from side to side (rotation)
D. Rotation of the thoracic spine
E. Elevation of the scapula
Answer: A. Nodding of the head, with some side bending and limited rotation
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Craniovertebral joints; Atlas (C1)
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the superior articular facet of the atlas and pin 6 marks the occipital condyles of the skull.
Together they form the paired atlanto-occipital joints, which allow nodding of the head, some side bending, and limited rotation.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Lumbar flexion and extension come from the sagittally oriented lumbar facets.
C. Rotation of the head happens mostly at the atlanto-axial joint, where C1 pivots around the dens of C2.
D. Thoracic rotation depends on the vertically oriented thoracic facets.
E. Scapular elevation is a muscle action, not a craniovertebral joint movement.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Pin 1 marks the tooth-like process projecting superiorly from the body of C2, which is the dens (odontoid process).
It's the distinguishing feature of the axis.
Why the other answers are wrong:

B. The anterior arch belongs to the atlas and wraps in front of the dens (pin 2 in the articulated view).

Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1771
A 24-year-old man is evaluated after a diving injury. Imaging shows a fracture at the base of the structure marked by pin 1 in the attached image. Which of the following movements depends most on this structure and its articulation?
![Illustration of the upper cervical vertebrae, posterosuperior and anterior views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-21-axis.png)
A. Rotation of the head, with C1 pivoting around C2
B. Elevation of the ribs
C. Lateral flexion of the thoracic spine
D. Flexion and extension of the lumbar spine
E. Nodding of the head at the atlanto-occipital joint
Answer: A. Rotation of the head, with C1 pivoting around C2
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Craniovertebral joints; Axis (C2) and the dens
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the dens of C2, which serves as a pivot for rotation of the head.
It articulates with the facet on the posterior surface of the anterior arch of the atlas, and the atlanto-axial joint allows C1 to pivot around it.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Rib elevation involves serratus posterior superior and the costovertebral joints.
C. Thoracic lateral flexion isn't related to the dens.
D. Lumbar flexion and extension come from the lumbar facet orientation.
E. Nodding happens at the atlanto-occipital joint between the occipital condyles and C1.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1772
A researcher studies the joints at the level shown in the attached image. How many synovial joints make up the atlanto-axial joint?
![Illustration of the upper cervical vertebrae, posterosuperior and anterior views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-21-axis.png)
A. Two, between the lateral masses of C1 and C2
B. Three, two between the lateral masses of C1 and C2 and one between the dens and the anterior arch of C1
C. One, between the dens and the anterior arch of C1
D. Four, two between the lateral masses and two between the dens and both arches of C1
E. None, because it's a fibrous joint
Answer: B. Three, two between the lateral masses of C1 and C2 and one between the dens and the anterior arch of C1
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Craniovertebral joints; Axis (C2) and the dens
Why this is correct:
The atlanto-axial joint is made up of three synovial joints: a paired joint between the lateral masses of C1 and C2, and a joint between the dens and the anterior arch of C1.
Together they let C1 pivot around the dens.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The paired lateral joints are only two of the three.
C. The dens joint is only one of the three.
D. The dens articulates with the anterior arch, not both arches.
E. All three are synovial joints.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1773
A first-year medical student is handed the vertebra shown in the attached image during a bone practical. It has a heart-shaped body, a long spinous process sloping downward, and facets on both its body and transverse processes. From which region does it come?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-23-thoracic.png)
A. Sacral
B. Thoracic
C. Lumbar
D. Cervical
E. Coccygeal
Answer: B. Thoracic
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Thoracic vertebrae: costal facets and facet orientation
Why this is correct:
A heart-shaped body with costal facets, transverse costal facets, a small round vertebral foramen, and a long spinous process sloping inferiorly are features of a thoracic vertebra.
The costal facets are where the ribs articulate.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Sacral vertebrae are fused into the sacrum.
C. Lumbar vertebrae have large bodies, short stout spinous processes, and no costal facets.
D. Cervical vertebrae have small bodies, bifid spinous processes, and transverse foramina.
E. Coccygeal vertebrae are rudimentary and fused.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1774

Question 1775

Question 1776
A researcher compares range of motion between spinal regions in cadavers. In one region, the articular facets are vertically oriented, which limits excessive flexion and extension but allows rotation and some lateral flexion. The vertebrae in this region look like the one in the attached image. Which region is this?
![Illustration of a vertebra, superior and lateral views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-23-thoracic.png)
A. Cervical
B. Sacral
C. Craniovertebral
D. Thoracic
E. Lumbar
Answer: D. Thoracic
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Thoracic vertebrae: costal facets and facet orientation
Why this is correct:
In the thoracic region the superior facets face posteriorly and slightly laterally and the inferior facets face anteriorly and slightly medially, a vertical orientation that limits flexion and extension.
The main movements are rotation and some lateral flexion.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Cervical facets are more horizontal and allow flexion, extension, and lateral flexion.
B. The sacral vertebrae are fused and don't move against each other.
C. Craniovertebral joints are specialized for nodding and rotation of the head.
E. Lumbar facets lie in the sagittal plane, allowing flexion and extension with limited rotation.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1777

Question 1781
A first-year medical student reviews the anterior view of the sacrum in the attached image. Which of the following passes through the openings marked by pin 2?
![Illustration of the sacrum, anterior and posterior views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-27-sacrum.png)
A. Vertebral arteries
B. Anterior rami of the sacral spinal nerves
C. Posterior rami of the sacral spinal nerves
D. The spinal cord
E. Meningeal branches only
Answer: B. Anterior rami of the sacral spinal nerves
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Sacrum: promontory, foramina, and hiatus
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the anterior (ventral) sacral foramina on the smooth pelvic surface of the sacrum.
They transmit the anterior rami of the sacral spinal nerves.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The vertebral arteries run through the cervical transverse foramina.
C. Posterior rami pass through the posterior sacral foramina (pin 3).
D. The spinal cord ends well above the sacrum, and the sacral canal is a continuation of the vertebral canal, not these foramina.
E. Meningeal branches travel back into the vertebral canal rather than out through these foramina.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1782
A first-year medical student reviews the posterior surface of the sacrum shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Photograph of the sacrum, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-sacrum-posterior.png)
A. Anterior sacral foramina
B. Intervertebral foramina
C. Sacral hiatus
D. Posterior sacral foramina
E. Transverse foramina
Answer: D. Posterior sacral foramina
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Sacrum: promontory, foramina, and hiatus
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks one of the paired openings on the rough, convex posterior surface of the sacrum, which are the posterior (dorsal) sacral foramina.
They transmit the posterior rami of the sacral spinal nerves.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The anterior sacral foramina are on the smooth pelvic surface, not the posterior surface shown here.
B. Intervertebral foramina are between unfused vertebrae higher in the column.
C. The sacral hiatus is the single midline opening at the inferior end (pin 4).
E. Transverse foramina are in cervical vertebrae.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1783
A first-year medical student reviews the posterior view of the sacrum in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?

A. Median sacral crest
B. Coccyx
C. Sacral hiatus
D. Posterior sacral foramen
E. Sacral promontory
Answer: C. Sacral hiatus
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Sacrum: promontory, foramina, and hiatus
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the inverted U-shaped opening at the inferior end of the posterior sacrum, which is the sacral hiatus.
It leads into the sacral canal, the inferior end of the vertebral canal.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The median sacral crest is the ridge in the posterior midline above the hiatus.

D. The posterior sacral foramina are the paired lateral openings (pin 3).

Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1784
A 31-year-old woman in labor is to receive caudal epidural anesthesia. The needle is placed through the opening marked by pin 4 in the attached image. Which space does the needle enter?
![Illustration of the sacrum, anterior and posterior views](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-27-sacrum.png)
A. The intervertebral disc between L5 and S1
B. The sacroiliac joint
C. The pelvic cavity anterior to the sacrum
D. The zygapophysial joint between L5 and S1
E. The sacral canal, the inferior end of the vertebral canal
Answer: E. The sacral canal, the inferior end of the vertebral canal
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Sacrum: promontory, foramina, and hiatus
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the sacral hiatus, an inverted U-shaped opening that leads into the sacral canal (the inferior end of the vertebral canal).
That's why the hiatus is a site for caudal epidural anesthesia.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The L5 to S1 disc is anterior and much higher.
B. The sacroiliac joint is lateral, at the auricular surface.
C. The pelvic cavity is reached through the anterior surface, not the posterior hiatus.
D. The L5 to S1 facet joint is at the superior articular facets, not the inferior end of the sacrum.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1785
A first-year medical student reviews the posterior view of the sacrum in the attached image. Which bone articulates with the surface marked by pin 2?

E. Coccyx

Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Sacral joints and the coccyx
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the auricular surface on the lateral side of the sacrum.
It articulates with the ilium of the hip bone to form the sacroiliac joint, through which the sacrum passes upper body weight to the pelvis.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The ischium doesn't articulate with the sacrum.
C. The femur articulates with the acetabulum of the hip bone, not the sacrum.
D. L5 articulates with the superior articular facets (pin 1) and the top of S1.
E. The coccyx joins the inferior tip of the sacrum.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1786

Why the other answers are wrong:

Question 1787
A first-year medical student reviews the anterior view of the bones shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Illustration of the sacrum, anterior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-29-coccyx.png)
A. Sacral promontory
B. Sacral hiatus
C. Ischium
D. Coccyx
E. Fifth lumbar vertebra
Answer: D. Coccyx
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Sacral joints and the coccyx
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the small, triangular bone at the inferior tip of the sacrum, which is the coccyx (tail bone).
It's formed by fusion of 3 to 5 rudimentary coccygeal vertebrae.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The sacral promontory is the anterior upper edge of S1.
B. The sacral hiatus is an opening on the posterior surface of the sacrum.
C. The ischium is part of the hip bone, lateral to the sacrum.
