Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1788
A researcher counts the vertebrae in an adult skeleton. Which of the following correctly lists the number of vertebrae in each region from superior to inferior?
A. 8 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 5 fused sacral, 3 to 5 fused coccygeal
B. 7 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 4 fused sacral, 1 coccygeal
C. 7 cervical, 12 thoracic, 4 lumbar, 6 fused sacral, 3 to 5 fused coccygeal
D. 7 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 5 fused sacral, 3 to 5 fused coccygeal
E. 7 cervical, 11 thoracic, 6 lumbar, 5 fused sacral, 3 to 5 fused coccygeal
Answer: D. 7 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 5 fused sacral, 3 to 5 fused coccygeal
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Vertebral column regions and functions
Why this is correct:
The vertebral column has 7 cervical, 12 thoracic, and 5 lumbar vertebrae, 5 sacral vertebrae fused into the sacrum, and 3 to 5 (usually 4) coccygeal vertebrae fused into the coccyx.
That makes about 33 vertebrae in all.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. There are 8 cervical spinal nerves but only 7 cervical vertebrae.
B. There are 5 sacral vertebrae, and the coccyx is 3 to 5 fused segments.
C. There are 5 lumbar and 5 sacral vertebrae.
E. There are 12 thoracic and 5 lumbar vertebrae.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1789
A researcher studies the bony origins of back muscles on the hip bone. Which of the following muscles arise in part from the iliac crest?
A. Rhomboid major and rhomboid minor
B. Latissimus dorsi and the erector spinae
C. Serratus posterior superior and serratus posterior inferior
D. Splenius and semispinalis
E. Trapezius and levator scapulae
Answer: B. Latissimus dorsi and the erector spinae
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Latissimus dorsi muscle; Erector spinae muscles
Why this is correct:
Latissimus dorsi arises from the lower thoracic spinous processes, thoracolumbar fascia, and iliac crest.
The erector spinae arise from a broad tendon attached to the posterior iliac crest, posterior sacrum, sacroiliac ligaments, and sacral and inferior lumbar spinous processes.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The rhomboids arise from spinous processes of C7 to T5.
C. The serratus posterior muscles arise from spinous processes and the nuchal ligament.
D. Splenius and semispinalis arise from the cervical and thoracic vertebrae and nuchal ligament.
E. Trapezius and levator scapulae arise from the skull and cervical and thoracic vertebrae.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1790
A first-year medical student reviews the sagittal section of the lumbar spine shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Illustration of the lumbar vertebral column, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-32-ligaments.png)
A. Annulus fibrosus
B. Posterior longitudinal ligament
C. Ligamentum flavum
D. Anterior longitudinal ligament
E. Supraspinous ligament
Answer: D. Anterior longitudinal ligament
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Anterior and posterior longitudinal ligaments and ligamentum flavum
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the broad fibrous band covering the front of the vertebral bodies and discs, which is the anterior longitudinal ligament.
It runs from the occipital bone to the sacrum and helps resist hyperextension.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The annulus fibrosus is the outer part of the disc itself, deep to this ligament.
B. The posterior longitudinal ligament runs inside the vertebral canal on the backs of the bodies (pin 1).
C. Ligamentum flavum joins adjacent laminae (pin 2).
E. The supraspinous ligament runs along the tips of the spinous processes.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1791
A 35-year-old woman is brought to the emergency department after her car was struck from behind. She has neck pain and stiffness. A whiplash injury is suspected, with severe stretching of the structure marked by pin 3 in the attached image. Which of the following motions does this structure normally resist?
![Illustration of the lumbar vertebral column, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-32-ligaments.png)
A. Posterior displacement of the intervertebral discs
B. Hyperflexion
C. Lateral flexion
D. Rotation
E. Hyperextension
Answer: E. Hyperextension
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Anterior and posterior longitudinal ligaments and ligamentum flavum
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the anterior longitudinal ligament, which lies in front of the vertebral bodies and prevents hyperextension.
In whiplash, the sudden back-and-forth motion causes severe hyperextension of the neck, stretching and sometimes tearing this ligament.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Posterior disc displacement is resisted by the posterior longitudinal ligament.
B. Hyperflexion is weakly resisted by the posterior longitudinal ligament and limited in the neck by the nuchal ligament.
C. Lateral flexion isn't the main motion resisted by the anterior longitudinal ligament.
D. Rotation is limited mainly by facet orientation.
Objective: Discuss the clinical presentation and anatomical basis for the following: spondylosis, spondylolisthesis and injuries associated with the vertebral column.

Question 1792
A first-year medical student reviews the sagittal section of the lumbar spine shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Illustration of the lumbar vertebral column, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-32-ligaments.png)
A. Ligamentum flavum
B. Interspinous ligament
C. Nucleus pulposus
D. Anterior longitudinal ligament
E. Posterior longitudinal ligament
Answer: E. Posterior longitudinal ligament
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Anterior and posterior longitudinal ligaments and ligamentum flavum
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the narrow ligament running along the posterior surfaces of the vertebral bodies inside the vertebral canal, which is the posterior longitudinal ligament.
It weakly resists hyperflexion and resists posterior displacement of the intervertebral discs.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Ligamentum flavum is on the posterior wall of the canal, joining the laminae (pin 2).
B. Interspinous ligaments connect adjacent spinous processes.
C. The nucleus pulposus is the gelatinous center of the disc.
D. The anterior longitudinal ligament covers the front of the bodies (pin 3).
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1793
A first-year medical student reviews the sagittal section of the lumbar spine shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Illustration of the lumbar vertebral column, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-32-ligaments.png)
A. Intertransverse ligament
B. Nuchal ligament
C. Posterior longitudinal ligament
D. Supraspinous ligament
E. Ligamentum flavum
Answer: E. Ligamentum flavum
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Anterior and posterior longitudinal ligaments and ligamentum flavum
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the broad, pale yellow fibrous tissue joining adjacent laminae on the posterior wall of the vertebral canal, which is the ligamentum flavum.
Thickening of this and other ligaments can contribute to spinal stenosis.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Intertransverse ligaments connect adjacent transverse processes.
B. The nuchal ligament is in the neck, from the external occipital protuberance to C7.
C. The posterior longitudinal ligament is on the anterior wall of the canal, along the vertebral bodies (pin 1).
D. The supraspinous ligament connects the tips of the spinous processes.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1794
A first-year medical student reviews the sagittal section of the lumbar spine shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Illustration of the lumbar vertebral column, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-34-spinous-ligaments.png)
A. Interspinous ligament
B. Anterior longitudinal ligament
C. Ligamentum flavum
D. Thoracolumbar fascia
E. Supraspinous ligament
Answer: E. Supraspinous ligament
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Supraspinous, interspinous, and nuchal ligaments
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the ligament running along the tips of the spinous processes, which is the supraspinous ligament.
It connects the spinous process tips from C7 to the sacrum and merges with the nuchal ligament above C7.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Interspinous ligaments are thinner and fill the space between adjacent spinous processes (pin 1).
B. The anterior longitudinal ligament is on the front of the vertebral bodies.
C. Ligamentum flavum joins laminae, anterior to the spinous processes.
D. The thoracolumbar fascia is a superficial aponeurosis, not a ligament along the spinous tips.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1795
A first-year medical student reviews the sagittal section of the lumbar spine shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Illustration of the lumbar vertebral column, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-34-spinous-ligaments.png)
A. Posterior longitudinal ligament
B. Supraspinous ligament
C. Ligamentum flavum
D. Intertransverse ligament
E. Interspinous ligament
Answer: E. Interspinous ligament
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Supraspinous, interspinous, and nuchal ligaments
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the thin ligaments filling the space between adjacent spinous processes, which are the interspinous ligaments.
They lie deep to the supraspinous ligament, which runs over the tips.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The posterior longitudinal ligament is inside the canal on the vertebral bodies.
B. The supraspinous ligament runs along the tips of the spinous processes (pin 2).
C. Ligamentum flavum joins the laminae, closer to the vertebral canal.
D. Intertransverse ligaments connect transverse processes, not spinous processes.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1796
A first-year medical student reviews the intervertebral disc shown in the attached image. Which of the following best describes the structure formed by pins 1 and 2 together, and the joint it creates?
![Illustration of a lumbar vertebra and intervertebral disc, superior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-37-iv-disc.png)
A. Vertebral body; a primary cartilaginous joint
B. Zygapophysial joint capsule; a plane synovial joint
C. Intervertebral disc; a symphysis (secondary cartilaginous joint)
D. Intervertebral disc; a plane synovial joint
E. Intervertebral disc; a fibrous joint
Answer: C. Intervertebral disc; a symphysis (secondary cartilaginous joint)
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Intervertebral disc structure
Why this is correct:
Pins 1 and 2 mark the two parts of an intervertebral disc, the nucleus pulposus and the annulus fibrosus.
The disc joins two vertebral bodies as a symphysis (secondary cartilaginous joint) and acts as a flexible shock absorber.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The image shows the disc on top of the body, and the joint is secondary, not primary, cartilaginous.
B. The facet joint capsule is between articular processes, not between vertebral bodies.
D. Plane synovial joints are the facet joints between articular processes.
E. The disc is a cartilaginous, not fibrous, joint.
Objective: Identify and describe the components and function of an intervertebral (IV) disc. Discuss the clinical implications of disc herniations.

Question 1797
A first-year medical student reviews the intervertebral disc shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Illustration of a lumbar vertebra and intervertebral disc, superior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-37-iv-disc.png)
A. Vertebral body
B. Posterior longitudinal ligament
C. Annulus fibrosus
D. Nucleus pulposus
E. Spinal cord
Answer: D. Nucleus pulposus
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Intervertebral disc structure
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the gelatinous center of the disc, which is the nucleus pulposus.
It has a high water content (about 90% in young people).
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The vertebral body lies beneath the disc.
B. The posterior longitudinal ligament runs behind the bodies inside the canal.
C. The annulus fibrosus is the outer ring of concentric fibrocartilage (pin 2).
E. The spinal cord is in the vertebral foramen, posterior to the disc.
Objective: Identify and describe the components and function of an intervertebral (IV) disc. Discuss the clinical implications of disc herniations.

Question 1798
A first-year medical student reviews the intervertebral disc shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Illustration of a lumbar vertebra and intervertebral disc, superior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-37-iv-disc.png)
A. Annulus fibrosus
B. Anterior longitudinal ligament
C. Vertebral body
D. Ligamentum flavum
E. Nucleus pulposus
Answer: A. Annulus fibrosus
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Intervertebral disc structure
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the outer ring of the disc, made of concentric layers of fibrocartilage, which is the annulus fibrosus.
Only its outer third is vascular and innervated in a healthy disc.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The anterior longitudinal ligament covers the front of the discs and bodies.
C. The vertebral body lies beneath the disc.
D. Ligamentum flavum joins the laminae posteriorly.
E. The nucleus pulposus is the gelatinous center (pin 1).
Objective: Identify and describe the components and function of an intervertebral (IV) disc. Discuss the clinical implications of disc herniations.

Question 1799
A 44-year-old man comes to the clinic because of low back pain that radiates down his left leg after lifting a heavy box. MRI shows that material from the structure marked by pin 1 in the attached image has protruded posterolaterally through the structure at pin 2 at the L4/L5 level. Which of the following spinal nerve roots is most likely compressed?
![Illustration of a lumbar vertebra and intervertebral disc, superior view](library/images/msk-back/back-lec-37-iv-disc.png)
A. L3
B. L4
C. S2
D. L5
E. S1
Answer: D. L5
Order: Third
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Herniated nucleus pulposus (L4/L5 example)
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the nucleus pulposus and pin 2 the annulus fibrosus, so this is a herniated disc.
Lumbar herniations usually go posterolaterally and affect the nerve root immediately inferior to the disc, so an L4/L5 herniation compresses the L5 root.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. L3 exits well above the L4/L5 disc.
B. The L4 root exits through the foramen above the L4/L5 disc and is usually spared by a posterolateral herniation.
C. S2 is too far below this level.
E. S1 is typically affected by an L5/S1 herniation.
Objective: Identify and describe the components and function of an intervertebral (IV) disc. Discuss the clinical implications of disc herniations.

Question 1800
A 50-year-old woman comes to the clinic because of neck pain radiating into her shoulder, arm, and hand. MRI shows a posterolateral herniation of the C5/C6 intervertebral disc. Which of the following spinal nerve roots is most likely compressed?
A. C5
B. C4
C. T1
D. C6
E. C7
Answer: D. C6
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Herniated nucleus pulposus (L4/L5 example)
Why this is correct:
Cervical spinal nerves exit superior to the vertebra of the same number, which keeps the same numerical relationship as in the lumbar spine.
A C5/C6 herniation compresses the C6 root, and a C6/C7 herniation compresses C7; these are the two most common cervical disc ruptures.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. C5 exits above the C5 vertebra, above the C5/C6 disc.
B. C4 exits above C5 and isn't at this level.
C. T1 is far below this level.
E. C7 is compressed by a C6/C7 herniation.
Objective: Identify and describe the components and function of an intervertebral (IV) disc. Discuss the clinical implications of disc herniations.

Question 1801
A researcher studies why most lumbar disc herniations extend posterolaterally rather than straight posteriorly. Which of the following best explains this pattern?
A. The supraspinous ligament pushes the disc laterally.
B. The annulus fibrosus is thinnest anteriorly, and the anterior longitudinal ligament is weak.
C. The annulus fibrosus is thin posterolaterally, and that region isn't supported by either longitudinal ligament.
D. The ligamentum flavum blocks herniation directly into the midline.
E. The nucleus pulposus sits in the anterior part of the disc.
Answer: C. The annulus fibrosus is thin posterolaterally, and that region isn't supported by either longitudinal ligament.
Order: Second
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Herniated nucleus pulposus (L4/L5 example)
Why this is correct:
Herniations usually extend posterolaterally because the annulus fibrosus is relatively thin there and receives no support from the anterior or posterior longitudinal ligaments.
The posterior longitudinal ligament resists straight posterior displacement of the disc in the midline.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The supraspinous ligament is on the spinous process tips, far from the disc.
B. Herniation is rare anteriorly, and the anterior longitudinal ligament is strong.
D. Ligamentum flavum joins the laminae and isn't what deflects disc material.
E. The nucleus pulposus is central, not anterior.
Objective: Identify and describe the components and function of an intervertebral (IV) disc. Discuss the clinical implications of disc herniations.

Question 1802
A 74-year-old man has had stiffness in his lower back for years. Imaging shows that his intervertebral discs have lost height. Which of the following changes in the disc best explains this with aging?
A. The disc converts into a plane synovial joint
B. The nucleus pulposus gains water and elastin, making it more turgid
C. The vertebral bodies fuse into a single bone
D. The nucleus pulposus dehydrates and loses elastin and proteoglycans while gaining collagen
E. The annulus fibrosus becomes fully vascularized and innervated
Answer: D. The nucleus pulposus dehydrates and loses elastin and proteoglycans while gaining collagen
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Age-related intervertebral disc changes
Why this is correct:
With age, the nucleus pulposus dehydrates and loses elastin and proteoglycans while gaining collagen, so the disc loses turgor and becomes stiffer.
The annulus fibrosus then carries more of the vertical load and often develops fissures, which is part of degenerative disc disease.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The disc stays a secondary cartilaginous joint.
B. This is the opposite of what happens with aging.
C. Vertebral bodies don't fuse with normal aging; only the sacral and coccygeal vertebrae are fused.
E. Only the outer third of the annulus is vascular and innervated, and aging doesn't change that into full vascularization.
Objective: Identify and describe the components and function of an intervertebral (IV) disc. Discuss the clinical implications of disc herniations.

Question 1803
A 19-year-old football lineman comes to the clinic because of low back pain. A lateral radiograph shows the L5 vertebral body displaced anteriorly on S1. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
A. Spinal stenosis
B. Spondylolysis
C. Kyphosis
D. Spondylolisthesis
E. Scoliosis
Answer: D. Spondylolisthesis
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Spondylolisthesis
Why this is correct:
Spondylolisthesis is displacement of one vertebra relative to another, most commonly at the L5 to S1 junction with L5 slipping anteriorly over S1.
Common causes are disc degeneration and sports injury.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Spinal stenosis is narrowing of the canal or foramina.
B. Spondylolysis is a fracture of the pars interarticularis without the slippage described here.
C. Kyphosis is an exaggerated thoracic curvature.
E. Scoliosis is a lateral curvature with vertebral rotation.
Objective: Discuss the clinical presentation and anatomical basis for the following: spondylosis, spondylolisthesis and injuries associated with the vertebral column.

Question 1804
A 29-year-old woman in her third trimester of pregnancy comes to the clinic because of low back pain. Examination shows an exaggerated inward curve of the lumbar spine with anterior rotation of the pelvis. Which of the following is the most likely finding?
A. Lordosis
B. Kyphosis
C. Scoliosis
D. Whiplash
E. Spondylolysis
Answer: A. Lordosis
Order: Second
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Abnormal vertebral curvatures: kyphosis, lordosis, and scoliosis
Why this is correct:
Lordosis (hollow or sway back) is an exaggerated lumbar curvature with anterior rotation of the pelvis.
Causes include weak trunk muscles, obesity, and pregnancy.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. Kyphosis is an exaggerated thoracic curvature.
C. Scoliosis is a lateral curvature with rotation of the vertebrae.
D. Whiplash is a hyperextension injury of the neck.
E. Spondylolysis is a pars interarticularis fracture.
Objective: Evaluate normal vertebral curvatures, and distinguish features of abnormal vertebral curvatures such as kyphosis, lordosis, and scoliosis.

Question 1805
A 13-year-old girl is seen for a school screening. When she bends forward, one side of her back is higher than the other. Radiographs show an increased lateral curvature of the thoracic spine with rotation of the vertebrae, and no congenital vertebral defects are found. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?
A. Spina bifida occulta
B. Lordosis
C. Kyphosis
D. Spondylolisthesis
E. Idiopathic scoliosis
Answer: E. Idiopathic scoliosis
Order: Second
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Abnormal vertebral curvatures: kyphosis, lordosis, and scoliosis
Why this is correct:
Scoliosis is an increased lateral curvature of the spine with rotation of the vertebrae.
With no congenital vertebral defect, the most likely cause is idiopathic.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Spina bifida occulta is a failure of the laminae to fuse.
B. Lordosis is an exaggerated lumbar curve.
C. Kyphosis is an exaggerated anterior-concave thoracic curve, not a lateral one.
D. Spondylolisthesis is slippage of one vertebra on another.
Objective: Evaluate normal vertebral curvatures, and distinguish features of abnormal vertebral curvatures such as kyphosis, lordosis, and scoliosis.

Question 1806
A researcher studies the normal curvatures of the adult vertebral column. Which of the following pairs of regions are secondary curvatures that are concave posteriorly?
A. Thoracic and sacral
B. Thoracic and lumbar
C. Cervical and lumbar
D. Lumbar and sacral
E. Cervical and thoracic
Answer: C. Cervical and lumbar
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Normal vertebral curvatures
Why this is correct:
The cervical and lumbar curvatures are secondary curvatures and are concave posteriorly.
The thoracic and sacral curvatures are concave anteriorly.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Thoracic and sacral curves are concave anteriorly.
B. The thoracic curve is concave anteriorly.
D. The sacral curve is concave anteriorly.
E. The thoracic curve is concave anteriorly.
Objective: Evaluate normal vertebral curvatures, and distinguish features of abnormal vertebral curvatures such as kyphosis, lordosis, and scoliosis.

Question 1807
A 66-year-old man with prostate cancer is found to have metastases in his lumbar vertebrae. Which of the following features of the venous drainage of the vertebral column best explains this spread?
A. The venous plexuses don't communicate with segmental veins of the trunk
B. The vertebral veins have strong valves that trap tumor cells
C. The vertebral column drains only into the vertebral artery
D. The internal and external vertebral venous plexuses lack valves and communicate with each other
E. The internal vertebral venous plexus lies outside the vertebral canal
Answer: D. The internal and external vertebral venous plexuses lack valves and communicate with each other
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Internal and external vertebral venous plexuses
Why this is correct:
The internal and external vertebral venous plexuses communicate with each other and lack valves.
That lets malignant cells and bacteria in the blood spread into the vertebral column.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The plexuses do drain into segmental veins of the trunk.
B. The plexuses lack valves.
C. Veins don't drain into arteries; the plexuses drain into vertebral veins of the neck and segmental veins of the trunk.
E. The internal plexus is within the vertebral canal, and the external plexus is outside it.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1808
A 40-year-old woman has localized back pain from a small tear in the outer annulus fibrosus of a lumbar disc. Which of the following nerves carries the pain from this structure?
A. Thoracodorsal nerve
B. Anterior rami forming the lumbar plexus
C. Posterior rami supplying the zygapophysial joints
D. Meningeal (recurrent meningeal) branches of the spinal nerves
E. Intercostal nerves
Answer: D. Meningeal (recurrent meningeal) branches of the spinal nerves
Order: Second
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Meningeal branches and vertebral column innervation
Why this is correct:
Meningeal branches of the spinal nerves supply the periosteum, the intervertebral discs (annulus fibrosus), the vertebral ligaments, and the meninges of the spinal cord.
Only the outer third of the annulus fibrosus is innervated in a healthy disc, so pain from an outer tear travels in these branches.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The thoracodorsal nerve supplies latissimus dorsi.
B. Anterior rami supply the limbs and body wall, not the disc.
C. Posterior rami branches supply the zygapophysial joints, not the disc.
E. Intercostal nerves run with the ribs.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1809
A first-year medical student reviews the dissection of the posterior neck shown in the attached image. Which of the following best describes the attachments of the structure marked by pin 2?
![Cadaveric dissection of the posterior neck](library/images/msk-back/back-lab-18-semispinalis.png)
A. Between adjacent transverse processes
B. From the external occipital protuberance to the spinous process of C7
C. From the tips of the spinous processes of C7 to the sacrum
D. Between the laminae of adjacent vertebrae
E. From the occipital bone to the sacrum along the front of the vertebral bodies
Answer: B. From the external occipital protuberance to the spinous process of C7
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Semispinalis muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the nuchal ligament, which spans from the external occipital protuberance to the C7 spinous process.
It merges with the supraspinous ligament, which continues from C7 down to the sacrum.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. This describes the intertransverse ligaments.
C. This describes the supraspinous ligament.
D. This describes the ligamentum flavum.
E. This describes the anterior longitudinal ligament.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1810
A first-year medical student examines the external surface of the bone shown in the attached image during a bone practical. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Photograph of the occipital bone, external view](library/images/msk-back/bas-occipital-external.png)
A. Inferior nuchal line
B. External occipital protuberance
C. Occipital condyle
D. External occipital crest
E. Superior nuchal line
Answer: E. Superior nuchal line
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Semispinalis muscle; Supraspinous, interspinous, and nuchal ligaments
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the curved ridge running laterally from the external occipital protuberance, which is the superior nuchal line.
Splenius capitis attaches here, and semispinalis attaches on the bone just below it.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The inferior nuchal line is the lower curved ridge, closer to the foramen magnum (pin 4).
B. The external occipital protuberance is the midline bump the line starts from (pin 1).
C. The occipital condyles sit beside the foramen magnum (pin 5).
D. The external occipital crest runs vertically in the midline from the protuberance toward the foramen magnum (pin 3).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1811
A first-year medical student examines the external surface of the bone shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?
![Photograph of the occipital bone, external view](library/images/msk-back/bas-occipital-external.png)
A. Mastoid process
B. Inferior nuchal line
C. Superior nuchal line
D. External occipital crest
E. Foramen magnum
Answer: B. Inferior nuchal line
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Semispinalis muscle; Supraspinous, interspinous, and nuchal ligaments
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the lower of the two curved ridges on the external occipital bone, between the superior nuchal line and the foramen magnum, which is the inferior nuchal line.
The area between it and the superior nuchal line receives semispinalis.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The mastoid process belongs to the temporal bone.
C. The superior nuchal line is the higher ridge (pin 2).
D. The external occipital crest is the vertical midline ridge (pin 3).
E. The foramen magnum is the large opening at the bottom of the bone.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1812
A researcher studies the region of bone between the structures marked by pins 2 and 4 in the attached image. Which of the following muscles attaches to this region, and what does it do when the right side contracts alone?
![Photograph of the occipital bone, external view](library/images/msk-back/bas-occipital-external.png)
A. Longissimus; laterally flexes the vertebral column to the right
B. Trapezius; elevates the right scapula
C. Semispinalis; extends the head and rotates it to the left
D. Splenius capitis; extends and rotates the head to the right
E. Semispinalis; extends the head and rotates it to the right
Answer: C. Semispinalis; extends the head and rotates it to the left
Order: Third
Difficulty: Hard
Reference: Semispinalis muscle
Why this is correct:
Pins 2 and 4 mark the superior and inferior nuchal lines, and semispinalis attaches to the occipital bone between them.
Semispinalis extends and contralaterally rotates the head, so the right muscle turns the head to the left.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Longissimus attaches to the mastoid process, not the occipital bone.
B. Trapezius arises from the occipital bone but acts on the scapula, and its attachment isn't the area between the nuchal lines.
D. Splenius capitis attaches to the superior nuchal line itself and rotates the head to the same side.
E. The muscle is right, but semispinalis rotates the head to the opposite side.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1813
A first-year medical student examines the external surface of the bone shown in the attached image. Which bone articulates with the structure marked by pin 5, and what movement does the joint mainly allow?
![Photograph of the occipital bone, external view](library/images/msk-back/bas-occipital-external.png)
A. Atlas; rotation of the head around the dens
B. Axis; rotation of the head
C. Atlas; nodding of the head
D. Mastoid process; side bending of the head
E. C7; flexion of the neck
Answer: C. Atlas; nodding of the head
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Atlas (C1); Craniovertebral joints
Why this is correct:
Pin 5 marks an occipital condyle beside the foramen magnum.
The occipital condyles articulate with the superior articular facets of the atlas (C1) at the atlanto-occipital joints, which allow nodding, some side bending, and limited rotation.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Rotation around the dens happens at the atlanto-axial joint, not at the condyles.
B. The axis doesn't articulate with the skull.
D. The mastoid process is part of the temporal bone, not an articulating vertebra.
E. C7 is far below the skull.
Objective: Identify and describe the normal structure and function of the vertebral column, its joints, and associated ligaments.

Question 1814
A first-year medical student examines the vertebra shown in the attached image, which has no body and no spinous process. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?
![Photograph of a vertebra, superior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-atlas-superior.png)
A. Superior articular facet
B. Vertebral foramen
C. Groove for the vertebral artery
D. Intervertebral foramen
E. Transverse foramen
Answer: E. Transverse foramen
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Vertebral artery and the transverse foramina
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the opening in the transverse process of the atlas, which is the transverse foramen.
The vertebral artery passes up through these foramina from C6 to C1 before running in the groove on the posterior arch of the atlas.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The superior articular facet is the large concave surface on the lateral mass (pin 2).
B. The vertebral foramen is the large central opening for the spinal cord (pin 1).
C. The groove for the vertebral artery is on the posterior arch (pin 3).
D. Intervertebral foramina are formed between two vertebrae.
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1815
A first-year medical student examines the posterior surface of the right scapula shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 7?
![Photograph of a scapula, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-scapula-posterior.png)
A. Glenoid cavity
B. Lateral border
C. Acromion
D. Superior angle
E. Inferior angle
Answer: E. Inferior angle
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 7 marks the lowest point of the scapula, where the medial and lateral borders meet, which is the inferior angle.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The glenoid cavity is at the lateral angle and faces the humerus.
B. The lateral border is the edge running up from the inferior angle toward the glenoid (pin 6).
C. The acromion is the lateral end of the spine at the tip of the shoulder (pin 3).
D. The superior angle is the upper medial corner (pin 1).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1816
A first-year medical student examines the posterior surface of the right scapula shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 6?
![Photograph of a scapula, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-scapula-posterior.png)
A. Inferior angle
B. Spine of the scapula
C. Superior border
D. Medial (vertebral) border
E. Lateral (axillary) border
Answer: E. Lateral (axillary) border
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 6 marks the thick edge running from the inferior angle up toward the glenoid cavity, on the side facing the armpit, which is the lateral (axillary) border.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The inferior angle is the lowest point (pin 7).
B. The spine is the ridge crossing the posterior surface (pin 4).
C. The superior border is the short upper edge next to the superior angle (pin 2).
D. The medial border is the opposite edge, facing the vertebral column (pin 5).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1817
A first-year medical student examines the posterior surface of the right scapula shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Photograph of a scapula, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-scapula-posterior.png)
A. Medial (vertebral) border
B. Spine of the scapula

D. Acromion

Answer: E. Superior border
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Levator scapulae muscle; Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the short, thin upper edge of the scapula running laterally from the superior angle, which is the superior border.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The medial border runs vertically along the side facing the vertebral column (pin 5).
B. The spine is the prominent ridge crossing the posterior surface below this edge (pin 4).
C. The lateral border runs from the inferior angle toward the glenoid (pin 6).

Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1818
A first-year medical student examines the posterior surface of the right scapula shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 5, and which muscle attaches to it below the level of the spine?
![Photograph of a scapula, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-scapula-posterior.png)
A. Medial border; rhomboid major
B. Lateral border; latissimus dorsi
C. Superior border; levator scapulae
D. Medial border; trapezius
E. Lateral border; rhomboid minor
Answer: A. Medial border; rhomboid major
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Rhomboid major and minor
Why this is correct:
Pin 5 marks the edge of the scapula facing the vertebral column, which is the medial (vertebral) border.
Rhomboid major attaches to the medial border between the spine and the inferior angle; rhomboid minor attaches at the level of the spine, and levator scapulae attaches above it.
Why the other answers are wrong:
B. The lateral border is on the opposite side, and latissimus dorsi attaches to the humerus.
C. Levator scapulae attaches to the superior angle and the medial border above the spine, not the superior border.
D. The border is right, but trapezius attaches to the spine and acromion.
E. Rhomboid minor attaches to the medial border at the spine, not the lateral border.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1819
A 36-year-old man is evaluated after a shoulder injury. The physician palpates the bony ridge marked by pin 4 in the attached image and the lateral process marked by pin 3. Which muscle attaches to both of these structures, and which nerve supplies it?
![Photograph of a scapula, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-scapula-posterior.png)
A. Trapezius; dorsal scapular nerve
B. Latissimus dorsi; thoracodorsal nerve
C. Trapezius; spinal accessory nerve (CN XI)
D. Rhomboid minor; dorsal scapular nerve
E. Levator scapulae; dorsal scapular nerve
Answer: C. Trapezius; spinal accessory nerve (CN XI)
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Trapezius muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the spine of the scapula and pin 3 marks the acromion.
Trapezius attaches distally to the lateral third of the clavicle, the acromion, and the spine of the scapula, and its motor supply is the spinal accessory nerve.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The muscle is right, but trapezius isn't supplied by the dorsal scapular nerve.
B. Latissimus dorsi attaches to the humerus.
D. Rhomboid minor attaches to the medial end of the spine, not the acromion.
E. Levator scapulae attaches to the superior angle and medial border.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1820
A first-year medical student examines the articulated pelvis shown in the attached image, seen from the left side. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Photograph of an articulated pelvis, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/bas-hip-bone-lateral.png)
A. Pubis
B. Iliac crest
C. Ischium
D. Acetabulum
E. Sacral promontory
Answer: B. Iliac crest
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Sacral joints and the coccyx
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the curved upper edge of the ilium, which is the iliac crest.
Latissimus dorsi and the erector spinae both arise in part from the iliac crest.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The pubis forms the anteroinferior part (pin 4).
C. The ischium forms the posteroinferior part of the hip bone (pin 6).
D. The acetabulum is the cup-shaped socket on the lateral surface (pin 3).
E. The sacral promontory is on the sacrum and faces anteriorly into the pelvis.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1821
A first-year medical student examines the articulated pelvis shown in the attached image, seen from the left side. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 2?
![Photograph of an articulated pelvis, lateral view](library/images/msk-back/bas-hip-bone-lateral.png)
A. Ischium
B. Sacrum
C. Pubis
D. Ilium
E. Acetabulum
Answer: D. Ilium
Order: First
Difficulty: Easy
Reference: Sacral joints and the coccyx
Why this is correct:
Pin 2 marks the large, flared upper part of the hip bone, which is the ilium.
The ilium articulates with the auricular surface of the sacrum at the sacroiliac joint.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The ischium is the posteroinferior part of the hip bone (pin 6).
B. The sacrum sits behind the hip bones and is seen only at the edge of this lateral view.
C. The pubis is the anteroinferior part (pin 4).
E. The acetabulum is the socket below the ilium (pin 3).
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1822

Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1825
A first-year medical student examines the posterior surface of the sacrum shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?

E. Auricular surface

Why the other answers are wrong:

Question 1826
A first-year medical student examines the posterior surface of the sacrum shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 4?
![Photograph of the sacrum, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-sacrum-posterior.png)
A. Vertebral foramen
B. Sacral hiatus
C. Intervertebral foramen
D. Median sacral crest
E. Posterior sacral foramen
Answer: B. Sacral hiatus
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Sacrum: promontory, foramina, and hiatus
Why this is correct:
Pin 4 marks the opening at the inferior end of the posterior sacrum, below the median sacral crest, which is the sacral hiatus.
It leads into the sacral canal and is a site for caudal epidural anesthesia.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. The sacrum has a sacral canal rather than a single vertebral foramen.
C. Intervertebral foramina are between unfused vertebrae above the sacrum.
D. The median sacral crest is the midline ridge above the hiatus (pin 3).
E. The posterior sacral foramina are the paired lateral openings (pin 2).
Objective: Differentiate the five regions of the vertebral column. Identify and describe the parts of a typical vertebra, and compare vertebrae from each region.

Question 1827
A first-year medical student examines the dissection of the upper back shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 1?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-back-serratus-post-sup.png)
A. Levator scapulae muscle
B. Rhomboid minor muscle
C. Spinalis muscle
D. Semispinalis muscle
E. Splenius muscle
Answer: E. Splenius muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Splenius muscle
Why this is correct:
Pin 1 marks the flat muscle on the back of the neck with fibers running up and laterally from the midline toward the skull, which is splenius.
It arises from the nuchal ligament and C7 to T6 spinous processes.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Levator scapulae runs down to the superior angle of the scapula, lateral to this field.
B. Rhomboid minor is at the base of the neck running toward the scapula and has been removed on this side.
C. Spinalis is the most medial column of the erector spinae in the thoracic region.
D. Semispinalis is deep to splenius and runs vertically.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1828
A researcher stimulates the muscle marked by pin 3 in the attached image in a cadaver model. Which of the following describes its innervation and proposed action?
![Cadaveric dissection of the back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-back-serratus-post-sup.png)
A. Posterior rami of spinal nerves; extends the vertebral column
B. 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves; elevates the ribs
C. Dorsal scapular nerve; retracts the scapula
D. 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves; depresses the ribs
E. 9th to 12th intercostal nerves; depresses the ribs
Answer: B. 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves; elevates the ribs
Order: Second
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Serratus posterior superior and inferior
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks serratus posterior superior, which runs from the nuchal ligament and C7 to T3 spinous processes to the superior borders of ribs 2 to 4.
It's innervated by the 2nd to 5th intercostal nerves and is thought to elevate the ribs and serve a proprioceptive role.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. This describes the erector spinae.
C. This describes the rhomboids.
D. The nerves are right, but this muscle elevates rather than depresses the ribs.
E. This describes serratus posterior inferior.
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.

Question 1829
A first-year medical student examines the dissection of the deep back shown in the attached image. Which of the following structures is indicated by pin 3?
![Cadaveric dissection of the deep back, posterior view](library/images/msk-back/bas-back-erector-spinae.png)
A. Iliocostalis muscle
B. Spinalis muscle
C. Serratus posterior inferior muscle
D. Longissimus muscle
E. Latissimus dorsi muscle
Answer: D. Longissimus muscle
Order: First
Difficulty: Medium
Reference: Erector spinae muscles
Why this is correct:
Pin 3 marks the intermediate column of the erector spinae, between iliocostalis laterally and spinalis medially, which is longissimus.
Why the other answers are wrong:
A. Iliocostalis is the lateral column over the ribs (pin 2).
B. Spinalis is the most medial column (pin 4).
C. Serratus posterior inferior is a thin intermediate muscle that has been removed here.
E. Latissimus dorsi is the large superficial muscle on the other side of the dissection (pin 5).
Objective: Identify & describe the superficial and deep back muscles, including their attachments, innervation, blood supply, major actions, and clinical testing. Predict the functional consequences of injury to or paralysis of these muscles.
